### Autoregressive regularized score-based diffusion models for multi-scenarios fluid flow prediction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24145v1)
- **Authors**: Wilfried Genuist, Éric Savin, Filippo Gatti, Didier Clouteau
- **Abstract**: Building on recent advances in scientific machine learning and generative modeling for computational fluid dynamics, we propose a conditional score-based diffusion model designed for multi-scenarios fluid flow prediction. Our model integrates an energy constraint rooted in the statistical properties of turbulent flows, improving prediction quality with minimal training, while enabling efficient sampling at low cost. The method features a simple and general architecture that requires no problem-specific design, supports plug-and-play enhancements, and enables fast and flexible solution generation. It also demonstrates an efficient conditioning mechanism that simplifies training across different scenarios without demanding a redesign of existing models. We further explore various stochastic differential equation formulations to demonstrate how thoughtful design choices enhance performance. We validate the proposed methodology through extensive experiments on complex fluid dynamics datasets encompassing a variety of flow regimes and configurations. Results demonstrate that our model consistently achieves stable, robust, and physically faithful predictions, even under challenging turbulent conditions. With properly tuned parameters, it achieves accurate results across multiple scenarios while preserving key physical and statistical properties. We present a comprehensive analysis of stochastic differential equation impact and discuss our approach across diverse fluid mechanics tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces an autoregressive regularized score-based diffusion model designed for predicting multi-scenario fluid flow. The model incorporates an energy constraint derived from turbulent flow statistics to enhance prediction quality and sampling efficiency. It features a general architecture, supports plug-and-play enhancements, and incorporates a conditioning mechanism for simplifying training across diverse scenarios. The study validates its approach through extensive experiments on complex fluid dynamics datasets encompassing various flow regimes and configurations, analyzing the impact of different stochastic differential equation (SDE) formulations. The results demonstrate stable, robust, and physically faithful predictions, even in challenging turbulent conditions, while preserving key physical and statistical properties.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies in combining several existing techniques in a specific way tailored for multi-scenario fluid flow prediction. While score-based diffusion models and autoregressive approaches have been used before, the integration of an energy constraint regularization derived from turbulent flow statistics seems to be a novel application. The paper also offers a relatively thorough comparative analysis of various SDE formulations in this context, which is a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper is significant because it addresses the challenging problem of accurate and efficient fluid flow prediction, especially in turbulent conditions. CFD simulations can be computationally expensive and maintaining long-term stability and accuracy remains difficult. The proposed model seeks to improve on these issues by using a generative model to quickly produce realistic solutions and improve the performance using a physically inspired regularization. Successfully generalizing to diverse flow configurations without extensive model redesign is also a valuable outcome.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The use of physically-motivated regularization is a strong point, potentially improving the realism and stability of predictions, and offering an alternative to completely data-driven approaches.
    *   The comprehensive experimentation across multiple datasets and flow regimes (transonic cylinder, turbulent radiative layer, MHD) lends credibility to the findings.
    *   The comparative analysis of different SDE formulations provides practical guidance for practitioners in the field.
    *   The simple and general architecture allows it to be easily implemented and applied to new data/problems with minimal effort.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While novel in its specific combination, the individual components are not entirely groundbreaking. The use of U-Net architectures with attention is fairly standard in generative modeling.
    *   The choice of physical/statistical constraints (regularization) is empirical and not necessarily generalizable. This means the regularization needs to be re-tuned for new cases to ensure good performance.
    *   The paper lacks a strong theoretical justification for the choice of energy constraint, or an analysis of how the regularization term affects model convergence and generalization.
    *   The authors are also not very critical in their discussion of why particular hyperparameters are optimized. The text is vague at times, indicating that values were selected based upon "established heuristics and insights from prior work."

*   **Impact:** If the proposed model proves to be broadly applicable and robust, it could have a significant impact on various applications of CFD, enabling faster design cycles, uncertainty quantification, and potentially real-time simulations. However, more extensive testing and comparison with other state-of-the-art methods (e.g., specialized turbulence models, physics-informed neural networks) are needed to establish its practical utility.

*   **Caveats:** It is important to consider that generative models can sometimes struggle to capture rare events or extreme values, which could be critical in certain fluid dynamics applications. Also, while the paper shows results for several scenarios, it doesn't provide extensive comparisons against existing state-of-the-art CFD methods or data-driven techniques, making it harder to assess the true performance gains.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper makes a worthwhile contribution to the field of scientific machine learning for fluid dynamics by presenting a practically useful model. Its strengths are in combining existing techniques in a meaningful way with good experimental analysis. The weaknesses lie in the lack of theoretical justification, some incremental novelty, and limited in-depth comparisons against the strongest competing methods. Further work demonstrating broader applicability, deeper theoretical understanding, and a rigorous quantification of improvement over established methods would justify a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Don't Just Follow MLLM Plans: Robust and Efficient Planning for Open-world Agents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24157v1)
- **Authors**: Seungjoon Lee, Suhwan Kim, Minhyeon Oh, Youngsik Yoon, Jungseul Ok
- **Abstract**: Developing autonomous agents capable of mastering complex, multi-step tasks in unpredictable, interactive environments presents a significant challenge. While Large Language Models (LLMs) offer promise for planning, existing approaches often rely on problematic internal knowledge or make unrealistic environmental assumptions. Although recent work explores learning planning knowledge, they still retain limitations due to partial reliance on external knowledge or impractical setups. Indeed, prior research has largely overlooked developing agents capable of acquiring planning knowledge from scratch, directly in realistic settings. While realizing this capability is necessary, it presents significant challenges, primarily achieving robustness given the substantial risk of incorporating LLMs' inaccurate knowledge. Moreover, efficiency is crucial for practicality as learning can demand prohibitive exploration. In response, we introduce Robust and Efficient Planning for Open-world Agents (REPOA), a novel framework designed to tackle these issues. REPOA features three key components: adaptive dependency learning and fine-grained failure-aware operation memory to enhance robustness to knowledge inaccuracies, and difficulty-based exploration to improve learning efficiency. Our evaluation in two established open-world testbeds demonstrates REPOA's robust and efficient planning, showcasing its capability to successfully obtain challenging late-game items that were beyond the reach of prior approaches.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Robust and Efficient Planning for Open-world Agents (REPOA), a novel framework designed to address the challenges of planning in complex, open-world environments with imperfect knowledge.  REPOA tackles the problem of agents relying on potentially inaccurate Large Language Model (LLM) knowledge by employing three key components: Adaptive Dependency Learning (ADL) to dynamically revise dependencies, Fine-grained Failure-aware Operation Memory (FFOM) to prevent repeated failures, and Difficulty-based Exploration (DEX) to improve learning efficiency.  The framework is evaluated in Minecraft environments (MineRL and MC-TextWorld), demonstrating the ability to acquire challenging late-game items and recover from inaccurate prior knowledge, outperforming existing approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the integration of three components to address the specific challenges of planning in open-world environments with inaccurate LLM knowledge. While each component individually has some precursors, their combined effect in REPOA, specifically ADL's approach to adapt to inaccuracies through a combination of plausibility heuristics (based on both failure history and analogy from similar items) and the FFOM mechanism, presents a novel solution.  The difficulty based exploration is also a contribution towards efficiency.  Existing work has focused on either pre-training or providing correct knowledge, or adopting less robust error-correction mechanisms.

*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a crucial limitation of LLM-based planning: the reliance on potentially flawed parametric knowledge. REPOA's ability to learn and adapt from scratch in realistic settings offers a pathway towards more robust and autonomous agents. The empirical results, demonstrating superior performance in challenging Minecraft tasks compared to existing methods (including learning-based and oracle baselines in the paper's Appendix) and in environments modified to induce knowledge conflicts, clearly show the significance of the contribution.  The success in acquiring previously unobtainable late-game items is a strong indicator of REPOA's potential. The paper demonstrates the efficacy of the approach using relatively smaller 7B LLM, contributing to an efficient and practical development direction.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined problem statement and clear articulation of the challenges.
    *   Novel integration of three key components that complement each other.
    *   Strong empirical evaluation across two established environments and multiple control conditions.
    *   Detailed ablation studies demonstrating the importance of each component.
    *   Analysis of failure cases and insights into why REPOA outperforms existing methods.
    *   The use of a smaller LLM model makes the approach potentially more accessible and practical.
    *   The experiments in modified environments shows that the algorithm effectively adapts to changes in knowledge.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The experimental setup, while extensive, is primarily limited to Minecraft environments. Generalizability to other complex open-world environments should be further explored.
    *   The paper notes the STEVE-1 controller often struggles with spatial goals, limiting effective experience collection.  This dependence may mean some exploration ability is left on the table.
    *   While providing code examples or a link to the codebase wasn't specified as something that had to be in the paper, not including it reduces the replicability and impact.
    *   The redstone challenge remains unsolved, suggesting potential limitations to REPOA's current knowledge acquisition mechanisms, and motivates future work in this aspect.
    * The design choices around the types of items selected in revisions might be overly simplistic and domain specific.

*   **Potential Influence:** REPOA has the potential to influence the design of future LLM-based agents in open-world environments. The emphasis on adaptive learning, failure awareness, and efficient exploration provides a valuable framework for building more robust and autonomous systems. The results suggest a promising direction for reducing reliance on pre-existing knowledge and enabling agents to learn effectively through interaction. The success from a 7B model also is promising towards using smaller LLMs.

*   **Score Justification:**

The paper has a notable significance due to its combination of novel techniques and empirically driven approach towards robust and efficient planning. Given its clear contribution and extensive empirical evaluation, a score of 7.5 is warranted. While it does not completely revolutionize the field, it addresses significant limitations of existing approaches and offers a promising direction for future research on planning with LLMs. The limitations around generalizability and some of the specific item revision design choices prevent it from being an even higher score, but the well designed experiments and analysis justify a score above the midpoint. The thorough evaluation provides ample evidence that the introduced methods perform well in the given settings and are well suited to the problem the authors set out to address.

### Threading Keyframe with Narratives: MLLMs as Strong Long Video Comprehenders
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24158v1)
- **Authors**: Bo Fang, Wenhao Wu, Qiangqiang Wu, Yuxin Song, Antoni B. Chan
- **Abstract**: Employing Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) for long video understanding remains a challenging problem due to the dilemma between the substantial number of video frames (i.e., visual tokens) versus the limited context length of language models. Traditional uniform sampling often leads to selection of irrelevant content, while post-training MLLMs on thousands of frames imposes a substantial computational burden. In this paper, we propose threading keyframes with narratives (Nar-KFC), a plug-and-play module to facilitate effective and efficient long video perception. Nar-KFC generally involves two collaborative steps. First, we formulate the keyframe selection process as an integer quadratic programming problem, jointly optimizing query-relevance and frame-diversity. To avoid its computational complexity, a customized greedy search strategy is designed as an efficient alternative. Second, to mitigate the temporal discontinuity caused by sparse keyframe sampling, we further introduce interleaved textual narratives generated from non-keyframes using off-the-shelf captioners. These narratives are inserted between keyframes based on their true temporal order, forming a coherent and compact representation. Nar-KFC thus serves as a temporal- and content-aware compression strategy that complements visual and textual modalities. Experimental results on multiple long-video benchmarks demonstrate that Nar-KFC significantly improves the performance of popular MLLMs. Code will be made publicly available.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Threading Keyframe with Narratives: MLLMs as Strong Long Video Comprehenders":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of applying Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) to long video understanding, given the limited context length of these models. The authors propose "Nar-KFC" (Narrating KeyFrames Captured), a plug-and-play module that facilitates effective and efficient long video perception. Nar-KFC comprises two steps:  First, it formulates keyframe selection as an integer quadratic programming problem, optimizing for both query-relevance and frame-diversity.  A greedy search strategy replaces the computationally complex IQP problem. Second, Nar-KFC introduces interleaved textual narratives generated from non-keyframes using off-the-shelf captioners to mitigate the temporal discontinuity introduced by sparse keyframe sampling. These narratives are inserted between keyframes in their temporal order, forming a more coherent representation.  The authors demonstrate that Nar-KFC improves the performance of popular MLLMs on multiple long-video benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The paper presents a reasonably novel approach that builds upon existing techniques in long video understanding. The combination of query-relevance, frame-diversity for keyframe selection *and* interleaved textual narratives from non-keyframes is the key novelty.  Prior work has looked at keyframe selection or textual summarization in isolation, but the integrated approach is a worthwhile contribution. However, both components are built from relatively well-established methods (greedy keyframe selection, off-the-shelf captioning). The novelty lies in the orchestration rather than invention of these individual methods.

**Significance:** The problem of applying MLLMs to long videos is significant, as current MLLMs struggle with the sheer volume of visual data.  The proposed Nar-KFC module directly addresses this issue in a training-free manner, making it readily applicable to various MLLMs. The experimental results demonstrate consistent improvements across different models and benchmarks, suggesting practical value. Specifically, improving performance over Video-MME on the challenging needle-in-a-haystack task in MLVU, which is notoriously difficult, is significant. A notable strength is the ease of integration--the plug-and-play nature is attractive. The method also compares well to related approaches like BOLT, with the main benefits appearing in more challenging videoQA scenarios. The authors have conducted extensive ablation studies, further bolstering the results.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dependence on Caption Quality:** The approach relies heavily on the quality of the off-the-shelf captioner. While the experiments explore different captioners, the best-performing ones are often the largest MLLMs which may limit the applicability on lower-resource settings. This is offset by the observation that the gains are modest and keyframes play a larger role.
*   **Computational Cost:** Although the greedy search is more efficient than solving the IQP, the process of extracting CLIP embeddings for all frames and generating captions can still be computationally demanding, especially for extremely long videos. While the narratives provide a good balance of computational cost and additional information, this cost should still be considered.
*   **Limited Theoretical Justification:** While the approach is well-motivated and empirically successful, there is limited deeper theoretical analysis beyond formulating keyframe selection as an IQP problem. Some of the design choices (e.g., balancing coefficient, narrative interval) are selected empirically, leaving room for further optimization with a stronger theoretical basis.
*   **Incremental Improvements:** The individual performance gains are noticeable but could be argued as incremental given how the base method combines fairly well-understood techniques. The integration of the method into video processing pipelines requires extra memory bandwidth and data transfer rates, factors which may be difficult to reconcile in low-resource situations.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a valuable engineering contribution for applying MLLMs to long video understanding. The keyframe selection with integrated narratives ("Nar-KFC") strategy is well-motivated, the method delivers good performance improvements on multiple benchmarks and is training-free, so easily adaptable. While the individual components are not groundbreaking, the integration of frame diversity, query-relevance and text narratives is unique and well-executed. The main limitations revolve around computational cost and potential over-reliance on the chosen captioning models. Despite these limitations, the practical significance and well-conducted experiments make this a solid and useful paper.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### LKD-KGC: Domain-Specific KG Construction via LLM-driven Knowledge Dependency Parsing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24163v1)
- **Authors**: Jiaqi Sun, Shiyou Qian, Zhangchi Han, Wei Li, Zelin Qian, Dingyu Yang, Jian Cao, Guangtao Xue
- **Abstract**: Knowledge Graphs (KGs) structure real-world entities and their relationships into triples, enhancing machine reasoning for various tasks. While domain-specific KGs offer substantial benefits, their manual construction is often inefficient and requires specialized knowledge. Recent approaches for knowledge graph construction (KGC) based on large language models (LLMs), such as schema-guided KGC and reference knowledge integration, have proven efficient. However, these methods are constrained by their reliance on manually defined schema, single-document processing, and public-domain references, making them less effective for domain-specific corpora that exhibit complex knowledge dependencies and specificity, as well as limited reference knowledge. To address these challenges, we propose LKD-KGC, a novel framework for unsupervised domain-specific KG construction. LKD-KGC autonomously analyzes document repositories to infer knowledge dependencies, determines optimal processing sequences via LLM driven prioritization, and autoregressively generates entity schema by integrating hierarchical inter-document contexts. This schema guides the unsupervised extraction of entities and relationships, eliminating reliance on predefined structures or external knowledge. Extensive experiments show that compared with state-of-the-art baselines, LKD-KGC generally achieves improvements of 10% to 20% in both precision and recall rate, demonstrating its potential in constructing high-quality domain-specific KGs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "LKD-KGC: Domain-Specific KG Construction via LLM-driven Knowledge Dependency Parsing":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LKD-KGC, a novel framework for constructing domain-specific knowledge graphs (KGs) from unstructured text using Large Language Models (LLMs).  LKD-KGC addresses the challenges of building high-quality KGs in specialized domains where knowledge is complex, hierarchical, and often lacks sufficient public reference material.  The framework has three main components: (1) Dependency Evaluation, which determines the optimal order of document access for knowledge base construction based on LLM-assessed comprehension priorities; (2) Schema Definition, which extracts and refines entity types and their definitions from both original documents and automatically generated abstracts; and (3) Triple Extraction, which identifies entities and relationships within the text guided by the defined entity schema. The authors claim that LKD-KGC, compared to state-of-the-art baselines, generally achieves improvements of 10%-20% in both precision and recall across several datasets, demonstrating its potential for constructing high-quality domain-specific KGs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its unsupervised approach to domain-specific KG construction, particularly in the automated hierarchical analysis of documents and the subsequent generation of a domain schema *without* relying on predefined schemas or extensive external knowledge bases. Prior works often rely on manually-defined schemas (RAP, CoT-Ontology) or external knowledge (AutoKG, KBTE, SAC-KG), making them less adaptable to closed or rapidly changing domains. The idea of leveraging LLMs to evaluate document dependencies and guide the processing order is a valuable contribution. While the individual components (document summarization, vector embeddings, LLM prompting for extraction) are not individually novel, their integration within LKD-KGC offers a unique approach.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the practical utility of constructing high-quality domain-specific KGs in areas where expert knowledge is crucial, but manual construction is inefficient, and external data is scarce.  A framework like LKD-KGC could be useful in areas such as enterprise knowledge management, scientific literature analysis, or regulatory compliance. The experimental results suggest a tangible performance improvement over existing unsupervised methods, adding to the significance. The claimed improvements of 10-20% in precision and recall are meaningful, especially if these gains are consistent across diverse domain datasets.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The method explicitly addresses the limitations of existing approaches in domain-specific KG construction.
    *   The unsupervised approach is a significant advantage, particularly for closed domains.
    *   The incorporation of hierarchical knowledge dependencies is a novel and insightful contribution.
    *   The use of LLMs for various sub-tasks (summarization, entity extraction, relationship extraction, dependency evaluation) is well-justified.
    *   The comprehensive experiments conducted on several datasets, as well as using two LLMs.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the paper claims improvements of 10-20%, the specific experimental results in Table 2 and Table 3 show that these results are not consistent across all datasets. A more detailed breakdown and analysis of these results would be helpful.
    *   The evaluation relies on LLM-as-judge for precision, which is a somewhat indirect method.  The accuracy of the LLM judge is crucial and should be discussed more extensively (perhaps with an inter-annotator agreement analysis).
    *   The paper does not dive into the computational cost or the efficiency of their framework.
    *   Although the code is made available in the anonymous repository, there is little mention about optimization techniques.
    *   The evaluation focuses solely on precision and recall.  Other relevant metrics, such as completeness, coherence, and consistency of the generated KGs, are not considered.

*   **Potential Influence:**  LKD-KGC has the potential to influence future research in KG construction by promoting the development of more adaptable and domain-aware methods. The automated schema generation approach is particularly promising. However, the impact will depend on the reproducibility and adaptability of LKD-KGC in other domains and on further addressing the limitations mentioned above. The detailed analysis of the experimental results would add to the impact.

*   **Rigorous rationale for the score:**  While LKD-KGC introduces a valuable, unsupervised and automates process for domain specific KG extraction without relying heavily on external sources, the framework relies heavily on LLMs, which is known to hallucinate, and the experimental section lacked specific analysis with LLM-as-judge. The reported improvements of 10-20% should be validated further to be more consistent. While LKD-KGC contributes a novel approach to automatically extract domain-specific KG, there is room for improvement in performance evaluation to raise the impact factor for future research.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Mixed-R1: Unified Reward Perspective For Reasoning Capability in Multimodal Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24164v1)
- **Authors**: Shilin Xu, Yanwei Li, Rui Yang, Tao Zhang, Yueyi Sun, Wei Chow, Linfeng Li, Hang Song, Qi Xu, Yunhai Tong, Xiangtai Li, Hao Fei
- **Abstract**: Recent works on large language models (LLMs) have successfully demonstrated the emergence of reasoning capabilities via reinforcement learning (RL). Although recent efforts leverage group relative policy optimization (GRPO) for MLLMs post-training, they constantly explore one specific aspect, such as grounding tasks, math problems, or chart analysis. There are no works that can leverage multi-source MLLM tasks for stable reinforcement learning. In this work, we present a unified perspective to solve this problem. We present Mixed-R1, a unified yet straightforward framework that contains a mixed reward function design (Mixed-Reward) and a mixed post-training dataset (Mixed-45K). We first design a data engine to select high-quality examples to build the Mixed-45K post-training dataset. Then, we present a Mixed-Reward design, which contains various reward functions for various MLLM tasks. In particular, it has four different reward functions: matching reward for binary answer or multiple-choice problems, chart reward for chart-aware datasets, IoU reward for grounding problems, and open-ended reward for long-form text responses such as caption datasets. To handle the various long-form text content, we propose a new open-ended reward named Bidirectional Max-Average Similarity (BMAS) by leveraging tokenizer embedding matching between the generated response and the ground truth. Extensive experiments show the effectiveness of our proposed method on various MLLMs, including Qwen2.5-VL and Intern-VL on various sizes. Our dataset and model are available at https://github.com/xushilin1/mixed-r1.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the "Mixed-R1: Unified Reward Perspective For Reasoning Capability in Multimodal Large Language Models" paper:

**Summary:**
The paper introduces Mixed-R1, a novel framework for enhancing the reasoning capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) through Reinforcement Learning (RL) post-training.  It addresses the limitation of previous RL-based MLLM training methods which typically focus on specific tasks. Mixed-R1 proposes a unified approach, combining a mixed reward function (Mixed-Reward) and a mixed post-training dataset (Mixed-45K) composed of diverse MLLM tasks. The key components are a data engine to create the Mixed-45K dataset and a Mixed-Reward design which includes: 1) matching reward for binary/multiple-choice problems, 2) chart reward for chart-aware tasks, 3) IoU reward for grounding, and 4) Bidirectional Max-Average Similarity (BMAS) reward for open-ended text responses. BMAS is introduced to handle long-form text by matching token embeddings between the generated response and the ground truth.  Experiments demonstrate performance gains on various MLLMs (Qwen2.5-VL and Intern-VL) across several benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of unifying the reward structure and post-training dataset for MLLMs to handle diverse tasks is a solid improvement over task-specific RL training. The BMAS reward function for open-ended responses is a valuable contribution as it avoids the need for an additional LLM judge. The Mixed-45K dataset creation methodology, employing the existing MLLM (Qwen2.5-VL) to filter the data, is a practical strategy. The paper addresses a clear gap in the existing literature, where most RL fine-tuning approaches are geared toward a narrow range of tasks.
*   **Significance:**  The ability to train MLLMs on a wider range of tasks using a unified framework holds significant potential. The results reported show measurable improvements on popular benchmarks. This work can influence future research directions on RL-based MLLM training.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The unification aspect is a major strength. It allows for a single model to improve across different reasoning tasks, which is more practical than task-specific models.
    *   The BMAS reward offers a computationally efficient and effective solution to evaluate open-ended generations.
    *   The detailed ablation studies, especially on reward combinations and the open-ended reward, provide valuable insights.
    *   The qualitative examples illustrate the improved reasoning of the model.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While BMAS addresses the cost of an extra LLM judge, its effectiveness depends on the base model's tokenizer and embedding quality. A more detailed analysis of BMAS's sensitivity to these factors could be beneficial.
    *   The choice of hyperparameters (especially the reward balancing factor λ) is briefly mentioned. A sensitivity analysis on these hyperparameters would strengthen the robustness claims.
    *   The results section focuses primarily on Qwen2.5-VL. While Intern-VL results are provided, more comprehensive experiments including other SOTA MLLMs would better demonstrate the generalizability of Mixed-R1.
    *   The paper does not compare the performance of RL post-training using the proposed method with other non-RL fine-tuning strategies for the same mixed dataset, potentially missing opportunities for a more complete evaluation.
    * The implementation is based on the Deepseek-R1 framework, so the contribution is more of an incremental improvement rather than completely new approach.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper presents a practical and effective approach for enhancing MLLMs through RL. The idea of unified reward and dataset design is likely to influence future research in this area. The BMAS reward function can be adopted in other contexts where open-ended text generation needs to be evaluated without relying on external LLM judges. The Mixed-45K dataset can serve as a valuable resource for MLLM research community, which is very helpful and will be adopted widely.

**Justification for the Score:**
Given the paper's novelty in unifying reward and dataset design for RL post-training of MLLMs, its strong experimental results, and the practical contribution of the BMAS reward and Mixed-45K dataset, the paper warrants a reasonably high score. However, some limitations pertaining to the hyperparameter tuning, more comprehensive MLLM evaluations, and lacking comparison with SFT method.

- **Score**: 7/10

### SCOUT: Teaching Pre-trained Language Models to Enhance Reasoning via Flow Chain-of-Thought
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24181v1)
- **Authors**: Guanghao Li, Wenhao Jiang, Mingfeng Chen, Yan Li, Hao Yu, Shuting Dong, Tao Ren, Ming Tang, Chun Yuan
- **Abstract**: Chain of Thought (CoT) prompting improves the reasoning performance of large language models (LLMs) by encouraging step by step thinking. However, CoT-based methods depend on intermediate reasoning steps, which limits scalability and generalization. Recent work explores recursive reasoning, where LLMs reuse internal layers across iterations to refine latent representations without explicit CoT supervision. While promising, these approaches often require costly pretraining and lack a principled framework for how reasoning should evolve across iterations. We address this gap by introducing Flow Chain of Thought (Flow CoT), a reasoning paradigm that models recursive inference as a progressive trajectory of latent cognitive states. Flow CoT frames each iteration as a distinct cognitive stage deepening reasoning across iterations without relying on manual supervision. To realize this, we propose SCOUT (Stepwise Cognitive Optimization Using Teachers), a lightweight fine tuning framework that enables Flow CoT style reasoning without the need for pretraining. SCOUT uses progressive distillation to align each iteration with a teacher of appropriate capacity, and a cross attention based retrospective module that integrates outputs from previous iterations while preserving the models original computation flow. Experiments across eight reasoning benchmarks show that SCOUT consistently improves both accuracy and explanation quality, achieving up to 1.8% gains under fine tuning. Qualitative analyses further reveal that SCOUT enables progressively deeper reasoning across iterations refining both belief formation and explanation granularity. These results not only validate the effectiveness of SCOUT, but also demonstrate the practical viability of Flow CoT as a scalable framework for enhancing reasoning in LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SCOUT: Teaching Pre-trained Language Models to Enhance Reasoning via Flow Chain-of-Thought."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Flow Chain-of-Thought (Flow CoT), a new reasoning paradigm for large language models (LLMs). Flow CoT models recursive inference as a progressive trajectory of latent cognitive states, where each iteration represents a refined reasoning stage.  To implement this, the authors propose SCOUT (Stepwise Cognitive Optimization Using Teachers), a lightweight fine-tuning framework that uses progressive distillation with multiple teacher models and a cross-attention-based retrospective module. SCOUT aims to enable Flow CoT-style reasoning in pre-trained LLMs without requiring extensive pretraining or manually labeled CoT data. The authors present experiments on eight reasoning benchmarks, demonstrating improvements in both accuracy and explanation quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the combination of several existing ideas into a cohesive framework, SCOUT, for achieving Flow CoT. The idea of recursive reasoning is not new, nor is progressive distillation or the use of cross-attention. However, the way the authors have integrated these components to create a practical and scalable method for enhancing reasoning in LLMs via fine-tuning is a valuable contribution. Flow CoT, as a reasoning paradigm, formalizes the concept of progressive iterative reasoning which helps build a more intuitive and structured understanding.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work is in its potential to address limitations of existing CoT methods and recursive reasoning approaches. SCOUT offers a way to improve reasoning performance without relying on curated step-by-step data (a major bottleneck for CoT) and avoids the need for expensive pretraining often associated with recursive reasoning. The demonstrated improvements on multiple benchmarks suggest that SCOUT is effective in enhancing reasoning capabilities, which is a crucial area of research for LLMs. Moreover, the emphasis on preserving the original computation flow during fine-tuning could make it more practical to adopt in real-world applications. The qualitative analysis supports the claim that SCOUT leads to deeper reasoning and better explanation quality, which is also significant. It is also notable that the authors provide extensive ablation and analysis to rigorously understand the effect of the different SCOUT components, further bolstering the significance of their contribution.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed methods and concepts.
    *   The experimental evaluation is comprehensive, covering a variety of reasoning benchmarks.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the importance of different components of SCOUT.
    *   The qualitative analysis adds further support to the effectiveness of SCOUT in improving reasoning quality.
    *   The method is practical and can be easily integrated into existing LLM pipelines.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the framework is novel, the individual components are relatively well-established. Therefore, the "breakthrough" factor might be considered incremental rather than revolutionary.
    *   The reliance on multiple teacher models could introduce complexity in practical deployments. While the paper demonstrates the feasibility of this, it might not be as straightforward for all users.
    *   The paper assumes that larger models are always better teachers. It would have been good to see experiments or discussion that touch on the scenario where this assumption isn't valid.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   SCOUT offers a promising approach to scaling reasoning in LLMs. It could inspire further research on similar fine-tuning frameworks that combine distillation, recursive refinement, and attention mechanisms.
    *   The Flow CoT paradigm could serve as a foundation for designing more structured and cognitively-motivated reasoning architectures.
    *   The simplicity and practicality of SCOUT could encourage wider adoption and exploration of recursive reasoning techniques within the LLM community.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper builds upon existing techniques, its key strength is the clever integration of these methods into a cohesive framework (SCOUT) that enables Flow CoT-style reasoning in LLMs through fine-tuning alone. The thorough experiments and analysis demonstrate the effectiveness and practicality of the proposed approach, which is an important step forward in enhancing the reasoning capabilities of LLMs. Although the individual components aren't radically new, the overall contribution is significant and has the potential to influence future research.

### Seeing is Not Reasoning: MVPBench for Graph-based Evaluation of Multi-path Visual Physical CoT
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24182v1)
- **Authors**: Zhuobai Dong, Junchao Yi, Ziyuan Zheng, Haochen Han, Xiangxi Zheng, Alex Jinpeng Wang, Fangming Liu, Linjie Li
- **Abstract**: Understanding the physical world - governed by laws of motion, spatial relations, and causality - poses a fundamental challenge for multimodal large language models (MLLMs). While recent advances such as OpenAI o3 and GPT-4o demonstrate impressive perceptual and reasoning capabilities, our investigation reveals these models struggle profoundly with visual physical reasoning, failing to grasp basic physical laws, spatial interactions, and causal effects in complex scenes. More importantly, they often fail to follow coherent reasoning chains grounded in visual evidence, especially when multiple steps are needed to arrive at the correct answer. To rigorously evaluate this capability, we introduce MVPBench, a curated benchmark designed to rigorously evaluate visual physical reasoning through the lens of visual chain-of-thought (CoT). Each example features interleaved multi-image inputs and demands not only the correct final answer but also a coherent, step-by-step reasoning path grounded in evolving visual cues. This setup mirrors how humans reason through real-world physical processes over time. To ensure fine-grained evaluation, we introduce a graph-based CoT consistency metric that verifies whether the reasoning path of model adheres to valid physical logic. Additionally, we minimize shortcut exploitation from text priors, encouraging models to rely on visual understanding. Experimental results reveal a concerning trend: even cutting-edge MLLMs exhibit poor visual reasoning accuracy and weak image-text alignment in physical domains. Surprisingly, RL-based post-training alignment - commonly believed to improve visual reasoning performance - often harms spatial reasoning, suggesting a need to rethink current fine-tuning practices.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Seeing is Not Reasoning: MVPBench for Graph-based Evaluation of Multi-path Visual Physical CoT" introduces MVPBench, a new benchmark for evaluating the visual physical reasoning capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs).  The benchmark features real-world visual physics, multi-image inputs to explicitly capture the chain-of-thought (CoT) based on changing visual evidence and multi-path CoT annotations representing diverse valid solution strategies.  The paper introduces a graph-based CoT consistency metric suite for fine-grained evaluation of reasoning fidelity and visual grounding. Surprisingly, their experiments reveal that state-of-the-art MLLMs struggle with visual physical reasoning, and that RL-based post-training, while commonly believed to improve visual reasoning, sometimes harms spatial reasoning performance in particular.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty of this work lies primarily in the creation of the MVPBench benchmark itself, rather than algorithmic innovation. Specifically, the key novel aspects are:
    *   Combining *real-world* visual physics, which is a critical component for real-world utility. CAD and Game-engine videos are very limited.
    *   Multi-image *visual evidence* alongside *chain-of-thought annotations*, forcing the model to link image features and reasoning.
    *   Multi-path CoT, enabling measurement of reasoning *path diversity*.
    *   Graph-based evaluation method for assessing CoT consistency.
    *   The finding that RL-based post-training can *hurt* spatial reasoning is genuinely surprising and challenges conventional wisdom, suggesting a need for vision-centric reward design. This is highly significant.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work stems from several factors:
    *   It reveals a significant gap in the physical reasoning abilities of even state-of-the-art MLLMs, highlighting that "seeing" an image does not necessarily translate to understanding the underlying physics.
    *   The finding regarding RL-based post-training raises important questions about current fine-tuning strategies and their impact on grounded reasoning.
    *   The paper introduces comprehensive visual reasoning evaluation in understanding physical world.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The benchmark is well-motivated by the limitations of existing datasets and evaluation methods.
    *   The multi-image, multi-path CoT annotation design is a significant improvement over single-image and single-path setups, allowing for more rigorous evaluation.
    *   The graph-based CoT consistency metric provides a valuable tool for assessing reasoning fidelity.
    *   The experimental results are compelling and challenge conventional wisdom.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The choice of relying on proprietary MLLMs (GPT-40) for some evaluation components (e.g., judging CoT steps) introduces a dependency and potential bias, given the opaque nature of these models.
    *   The dataset might be limited in domain coverage despite the efforts to cover four categories.
    *   The reliance on GPT-4 for generating scene descriptions in the experiments on the physics experiments might introduce a language bias. Though the paper states that textual cues were minimized in the initial design of the task, the subsequent introduction of GPT-4-generated descriptions could inadvertently lead the models to rely on these textual priors.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence:
    *   The development of new MLLMs with improved visual physical reasoning capabilities.
    *   The design of better fine-tuning strategies that prioritize grounded reasoning and coherence.
    *   The creation of more robust evaluation metrics for multimodal reasoning tasks.
    *   Future work on understanding and mitigating the limitations of RL-based post-training.

*   **Justification for Score:**

The paper offers a significant contribution to the field by rigorously highlighting the gap in visual physical reasoning within existing MLLMs. The construction of the MVPBench and the surprising results regarding RL fine-tuning are both novel and critical.  However, the reliance on black-box systems for evaluation and the dataset limitations (domain coverage) are weaknesses. These weaknesses hold back the score.
Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### CodeV-R1: Reasoning-Enhanced Verilog Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24183v1)
- **Authors**: Yaoyu Zhu, Di Huang, Hanqi Lyu, Xiaoyun Zhang, Chongxiao Li, Wenxuan Shi, Yutong Wu, Jianan Mu, Jinghua Wang, Yang Zhao, Pengwei Jin, Shuyao Cheng, Shengwen Liang, Xishan Zhang, Rui Zhang, Zidong Du, Qi Guo, Xing Hu, Yunji Chen
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) trained via reinforcement learning with verifiable reward (RLVR) have achieved breakthroughs on tasks with explicit, automatable verification, such as software programming and mathematical problems. Extending RLVR to electronic design automation (EDA), especially automatically generating hardware description languages (HDLs) like Verilog from natural-language (NL) specifications, however, poses three key challenges: the lack of automated and accurate verification environments, the scarcity of high-quality NL-code pairs, and the prohibitive computation cost of RLVR. To this end, we introduce CodeV-R1, an RLVR framework for training Verilog generation LLMs. First, we develop a rule-based testbench generator that performs robust equivalence checking against golden references. Second, we propose a round-trip data synthesis method that pairs open-source Verilog snippets with LLM-generated NL descriptions, verifies code-NL-code consistency via the generated testbench, and filters out inequivalent examples to yield a high-quality dataset. Third, we employ a two-stage "distill-then-RL" training pipeline: distillation for the cold start of reasoning abilities, followed by adaptive DAPO, our novel RLVR algorithm that can reduce training cost by adaptively adjusting sampling rate. The resulting model, CodeV-R1-7B, achieves 68.6% and 72.9% pass@1 on VerilogEval v2 and RTLLM v1.1, respectively, surpassing prior state-of-the-art by 12~20%, while matching or even exceeding the performance of 671B DeepSeek-R1. We will release our model, training pipeline, and dataset to facilitate research in EDA and LLM communities.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the CodeV-R1 paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CodeV-R1, a reinforcement learning with verifiable reward (RLVR) framework specifically designed for training large language models (LLMs) to generate Verilog code from natural language specifications.  It addresses three key challenges in applying RLVR to hardware design: the lack of automated verification environments, the scarcity of high-quality NL-code pairs, and the high computational cost of RLVR.  The framework includes a rule-based testbench generator for equivalence checking, a round-trip data synthesis method that generates NL-code pairs by filtering LLM-generated descriptions of open-source Verilog snippets, and a two-stage training pipeline ("distill-then-RL") incorporating an adaptive DAPO (Dynamic Average Policy Optimization) algorithm to reduce training costs. The resulting model, CodeV-R1-7B, achieves state-of-the-art performance on VerilogEval v2 and RTLLM benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates significant novelty in several areas.

    *   *Automated Testbench Generation:* Developing a rule-based testbench generator for Verilog is a valuable contribution in itself. It addresses a long-standing bottleneck in hardware design automation. The authors present convincing evidence for the superiority of their testbench compared to LLM-generated testbenches, showing a key improvement.
    *   *Round-Trip Data Synthesis:* The round-trip data synthesis approach is also novel and elegant. It's an effective method for creating a high-quality NL-code dataset, mitigating the data scarcity problem that plagues hardware design. The use of LLMs to bootstrap this process is clever, although reliant on the quality of those initial LLM outputs.
    *   *Adaptive DAPO:*  Extending DAPO with an adaptive mechanism to adjust the sampling rate based on discard rates is an important optimization. It directly tackles the computational cost challenge by reducing wasted sampling efforts.
    *   *Application to RTL Generation:* Applying RLVR to RTL generation is a less explored area than, for example, the area of Software RTL generation, or mathematical problem solving, though there has been prior work. The choice of the Verilog domain provides a unique set of challenges (verification, data scarcity) that the paper addresses well.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its potential to automate and improve hardware design processes.

    *   *Performance Improvement:* The empirical results convincingly demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on standard Verilog benchmarks. This represents a tangible step towards reliable and automated hardware generation.
    *   *Framework Contribution:* CodeV-R1 is more than just a model; it's a comprehensive framework that can be used by other researchers to train Verilog generation LLMs. The release of the model, training pipeline, and dataset will accelerate research in this area.
    *   *Impact on EDA:* The framework has the potential to impact the broader EDA field. The methods for addressing verification and data scarcity are generalizable and could influence other hardware design automation tasks.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   *Comprehensive Approach:* The paper addresses all three major challenges hindering RLVR for Verilog generation in a cohesive and well-engineered system.
    *   *Strong Empirical Results:* The performance gains over previous state-of-the-art models are significant and well-supported by experiments on multiple benchmarks.
    *   *Detailed Ablation Studies:* The paper includes detailed ablation studies demonstrating the effectiveness of equivalence checking, difficulty filtering, and the adaptive DAPO algorithm.
    *   *Clear Presentation:* The paper is well-written and clearly explains complex concepts.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   *Dependency on Foundation Models:* The framework relies on the quality of foundation models like DeepSeek-R1 and Qwen2.5-Coder. Improvements in these models will likely translate to improvements in CodeV-R1, but also makes it somewhat tied to these particular models.
    *   *Limited Scope of Verilog Design Challenges:* While the paper addresses many challenges, it mainly focuses on the functionality of generated Verilog code. It touches briefly on PPA, but the framework does not explicitly target power, performance, and area optimizations.
    *   *Hardware Limitations:* Some implementation details require high-end hardware resources which could be a hindrance to certain researchers.

*   **Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a strong and original contribution to the field of hardware generation. The framework is thoughtfully designed, the empirical results are compelling, and the release of the model and dataset will foster further research. While the dependency on foundation models and the limited scope of Verilog design challenges prevent it from being a perfect score, CodeV-R1 represents a significant advance. The combination of novel techniques and clear improvements pushes RTL generation forward in a meaningful way.

### Beyond Exponential Decay: Rethinking Error Accumulation in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24187v1)
- **Authors**: Mikhail L. Arbuzov, Alexey A. Shvets, Sisong Beir
- **Abstract**: The prevailing assumption of an exponential decay in large language model (LLM) reliability with sequence length, predicated on independent per-token error probabilities, posits an inherent limitation for long autoregressive outputs. Our research fundamentally challenges this view by synthesizing emerging evidence that LLM errors are not uniformly distributed but are concentrated at sparse "key tokens" ($5-10\%$ of total tokens) representing critical decision junctions. By distinguishing these high-impact tokens from the increasingly predictable majority, we introduce a new reliability formula explaining the sustained coherence of modern LLMs over thousands of tokens. Converging research streams reveal that long-context performance primarily depends on accurately navigating a few crucial semantic decision points rather than on uniform token-level accuracy, enabling targeted strategies that significantly outperform brute-force approaches. We thus propose a framework for next-generation systems centered on selective preservation of semantically vital tokens, dynamic computational allocation at uncertain decision boundaries, multi-path exploration at ambiguities, and architectures aligned with natural semantic domains. This marks a fundamental shift from raw scaling to strategic reasoning, promising breakthrough performance without proportionate computational scaling and offering a more nuanced understanding that supersedes the exponential decay hypothesis, thereby opening pathways toward substantially more powerful and efficient language systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper challenges the prevailing view that Large Language Model (LLM) reliability degrades exponentially with sequence length due to independent per-token error probabilities. It proposes an alternative framework arguing that LLM errors are concentrated in a small subset of "key tokens" representing critical decision junctions. The authors suggest a new reliability formula that incorporates the concepts of key and non-key tokens, stratified manifolds of representations (semantic clusters), and self-consistency.  They argue that long-context performance hinges on accurately navigating a few key decision points, enabling targeted strategies that outperform brute-force scaling. They propose a new system design focused on selective preservation of vital tokens, dynamic computation allocation, multi-path exploration, and architectures aligned with semantic domains. This promises performance breakthroughs without proportionate computational scaling.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a compelling and well-supported argument against the simplistic "exponential decay" model of LLM reliability.  It synthesizes several strands of recent research—attention patterns, embedding geometry, multi-path reasoning, long-context performance, and tool integration—into a coherent framework.  The paper effectively demonstrates that errors are not uniformly distributed and that long-range context utilization is far from equal across all tokens.  The proposed framework based on "key tokens," stratified manifolds, and self-consistency offers a more nuanced and realistic understanding of LLM behavior.

**Strengths:**

*   **Strong Synthesis:** The paper masterfully brings together diverse findings from recent literature, providing a holistic perspective on error accumulation in LLMs.
*   **Clear Theoretical Framework:** The proposed framework is well-defined and provides a concrete alternative to the existing exponential decay model. The refined formula is more realistic given empirical evidence.
*   **Practical Implications:** The paper outlines numerous practical implications for prompt engineering, model architecture, and reliability evaluation, suggesting new avenues for improving LLM performance and efficiency.
*   **Comprehensive Empirical Evidence:** The paper provides substantial empirical evidence to support its claims, drawing from a wide range of recent studies.
*   **Challenges Conventional Wisdom:** It convincingly challenges the "autoregressive LLMs are doomed" narrative by demonstrating that targeted approaches can achieve significant performance gains.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Qualitative Nature:** While the arguments are strong and supported by evidence, some aspects of the framework remain qualitative.  The precise determination of "key tokens" and the dynamics of manifold transitions require further formalization. The exact nature of k(n), specifically its dependencies, is not sufficiently described.
*   **Lack of Novel Empirical Results:** The paper primarily synthesizes existing research rather than presenting novel empirical results. While the synthesis is valuable, original experiments could have strengthened the claims.
*   **Oversimplification:** While the paper provides a more nuanced model than the simplistic exponential decay, it's still a simplification of the complex inner workings of LLMs.  There are likely other factors contributing to error accumulation that are not fully captured in the proposed framework.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper's novelty lies in its holistic synthesis of existing research and its development of a coherent framework that challenges the prevailing exponential decay model. It provides a more nuanced and actionable perspective on LLM reliability, paving the way for more efficient and effective system designs. The paper's potential significance is high, as it could significantly influence the direction of future research in LLMs, particularly in the areas of long-context modeling, attention mechanisms, and modular architectures. The emphasis on strategic reasoning over brute-force scaling is a significant and potentially transformative idea.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution by synthesizing a substantial body of recent work and reframing the understanding of error accumulation in LLMs. It successfully critiques the simplistic exponential decay model and proposes a more realistic alternative. While lacking original empirical data, the paper's comprehensive synthesis and insightful analysis are invaluable. It presents a clear path for future research and development, moving the field beyond the limitations of raw scaling. Therefore, a score of 8 is warranted.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Fine-Tune an SLM or Prompt an LLM? The Case of Generating Low-Code Workflows
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24189v1)
- **Authors**: Orlando Marquez Ayala, Patrice Bechard, Emily Chen, Maggie Baird, Jingfei Chen
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) such as GPT-4o can handle a wide range of complex tasks with the right prompt. As per token costs are reduced, the advantages of fine-tuning Small Language Models (SLMs) for real-world applications -- faster inference, lower costs -- may no longer be clear. In this work, we present evidence that, for domain-specific tasks that require structured outputs, SLMs still have a quality advantage. We compare fine-tuning an SLM against prompting LLMs on the task of generating low-code workflows in JSON form. We observe that while a good prompt can yield reasonable results, fine-tuning improves quality by 10% on average. We also perform systematic error analysis to reveal model limitations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates whether fine-tuning a small language model (SLM) or prompting a large language model (LLM) is more effective for generating low-code workflows. They create a pipeline that decomposes workflow generation into sub-tasks: outline generation and input population. They use retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) and compare fine-tuning a Mistral-Nemo-12B-Base SLM against prompting several LLMs (GPT-40-mini, GPT-40, Gemini-2.0-Flash, Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct).  The evaluation utilizes a custom metric, Flow Similarity (FlowSim), and a manual error analysis to assess model performance. The key finding is that fine-tuning the SLM yields better results than prompting LLMs, especially in domain-specific tasks requiring structured outputs, despite the larger size of the LLMs.  They also identify specific areas where each type of model excels and falls short through systematic error analysis.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** While the general comparison between fine-tuning and prompting is not entirely new, this paper's contribution lies in applying this comparison to a specific, practical, and complex task (low-code workflow generation) and performing a detailed error analysis. The custom `FlowSim` metric, while having limitations, is a valuable contribution given the structured output nature of the workflows.  The decomposition of the workflow generation and the RAG integration add a layer of engineering value. They contribute a comprehensive workflow pipeline.

* **Significance:**  The paper addresses a relevant problem in the field of generative AI and low-code/no-code development. The conclusion that fine-tuning SLMs can outperform prompting LLMs in specific domain-oriented scenarios is significant because it challenges the prevailing notion that simply scaling up model size is always the best solution.  It also argues against solely focusing on the latest generation of LLMs. If replicated, this finding has practical implications for resource-constrained environments or situations where specific domain knowledge is crucial. Their results suggests a direction that benefits enterprises who possess training data. However, the generalizability to other domain-specific tasks is something that would need further evidence.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Well-defined problem:** The paper tackles a clearly defined and industrially relevant task.
    * **Comprehensive evaluation:** It employs both quantitative (FlowSim) and qualitative (error analysis) methods for evaluation, providing a more nuanced understanding of model performance.
    * **Systematic error analysis:** The paper goes beyond simple accuracy metrics and conducts a thorough error analysis to identify strengths and weaknesses of each model type, providing insights for future improvements.
    * **RAG integration:** Their adoption of a RAG pipeline is well justified.
    * **Clear exposition:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Scope:** The study is focused on a single enterprise system and specific types of workflows. While this provides depth, the generalizability of the findings to other domains or systems remains to be demonstrated.
    * **FlowSim Metric Limitations:**  The paper acknowledges the limitations of the FlowSim metric (only comparing against one reference, requiring exact string matches, ignoring structure errors). While structure error calculations are included, a more robust metric would improve the evaluation. Addressing the "one reference" workflow aspect would enhance the metric's credibility.
    * **Dataset Dependency:** The conclusions regarding SLM superiority are heavily reliant on the quality and relevance of the fine-tuning dataset. Creating and labeling datasets can be costly and time-consuming. The study does not provide a deep explanation for dataset extraction and labeling process.
    * **Specific Models:** The conclusions are tied to specific models and model architectures. While the principle might hold true for other models, it needs to be confirmed.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence the design of generative AI applications in enterprise settings, encouraging developers to consider fine-tuning SLMs alongside prompting LLMs, especially where structured output and domain-specific knowledge are crucial. The systematic error analysis approach can serve as a guide for other researchers in evaluating similar tasks.

* **Justification for Score:**  The paper makes a tangible contribution to the current state of art by providing a comparative analysis. They were able to provide an error analysis methodology and provide a strong argument in favor of SLMs in a narrowly defined task.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### CLaSp: In-Context Layer Skip for Self-Speculative Decoding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24196v1)
- **Authors**: Longze Chen, Renke Shan, Huiming Wang, Lu Wang, Ziqiang Liu, Run Luo, Jiawei Wang, Hamid Alinejad-Rokny, Min Yang
- **Abstract**: Speculative decoding (SD) is a promising method for accelerating the decoding process of Large Language Models (LLMs). The efficiency of SD primarily hinges on the consistency between the draft model and the verify model. However, existing drafting approaches typically require additional modules to be trained, which can be challenging to implement and ensure compatibility across various LLMs. In this paper, we propose CLaSp, an in-context layer-skipping strategy for self-speculative decoding. Unlike prior methods, CLaSp does not require additional drafting modules or extra training. Instead, it employs a plug-and-play mechanism by skipping intermediate layers of the verify model to construct a compressed draft model. Specifically, we develop a dynamic programming algorithm that optimizes the layer-skipping process by leveraging the complete hidden states from the last verification stage as an objective. This enables CLaSp to dynamically adjust its layer-skipping strategy after each verification stage, without relying on pre-optimized sets of skipped layers. Experimental results across diverse downstream tasks demonstrate that CLaSp achieves a speedup of 1.3x ~ 1.7x on LLaMA3 series models without altering the original distribution of the generated text.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CLaSp: In-Context Layer Skip for Self-Speculative Decoding":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CLaSp, a novel self-speculative decoding method for accelerating Large Language Model (LLM) inference. Unlike prior speculative decoding approaches that require training additional drafting modules or complex pre-optimization, CLaSp uses an in-context, dynamic layer-skipping strategy.  It essentially creates a compressed "draft" model by intelligently skipping intermediate layers of the original verify model.  A dynamic programming algorithm is used to optimize the selection of layers to skip at each decoding step, based on the hidden states of the last accepted token. The paper demonstrates that CLaSp achieves significant speedups (1.3x-1.7x on LLaMA3 models) on various tasks without altering the output distribution.  The approach is presented as a plug-and-play method, improving the efficiency of LLM inference.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of dynamic, in-context layer skipping for *self*-speculative decoding is reasonably novel.  While layer skipping itself is not new, applying it *dynamically* based on the current context to a *self*-speculative decoding framework is a contribution. The use of dynamic programming to optimize layer skipping is also a positive. Compared to Bayesian Optimization required in Self-SD, it seems to offer more real-time adaptability. However, it's important to note that approaches like SWIFT already explored dynamic layer adaptation but using different strategies, requiring some hyperparameter tuning based on number of user requests and not necessarily adapting to the token-level context.

*   **Significance:**  Accelerating LLM inference is a crucial problem, and speculative decoding is a promising solution. CLaSp's potential to improve inference speed without the need for retraining or pre-optimization makes it potentially useful and significant. The observed speedups are also meaningful and practically beneficial, especially for large models. The "plug-and-play" nature of CLaSp enhances its appeal, as it can be easily integrated into existing systems.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Training-free and plug-and-play:** This is a major advantage, simplifying deployment and compatibility.
    *   **Dynamic Adaptation:** The ability to adjust the layer skipping strategy based on context is a key differentiator and allows the model to adapt better to varying task requirements.
    *   **Reasonable Speedups:** The reported 1.3x-1.7x speedups are significant and demonstrate the practical utility of the approach.
    *   **Sparse persistence observations:** the idea of sparse persistence shows how this method can reduce the number of layers for optimisation and make the system more efficient.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on Model Architecture:** The effectiveness of layer skipping and the optimal number of layers to skip are highly dependent on the specific LLM architecture. The hyperparameter tuning experiments reported partially mitigate this, yet further validation with more diverse LLM families would strengthen the claims of general applicability.
    *   **Computational Overhead:** While the authors address the overhead of layer optimization through parallelization strategies, this component may still pose a bottleneck for resource-constrained devices.
    *   **Limited Comparison:** the inclusion of SWIFT and Self-SD provides comparison, but more speculative decoding alternatives, especially those that adapt the layer skipping, would make a more solid and well-justified comparison.
    *   **Limited Ablation Study:** more ablation studies on the impact of the algorithm would be relevant to support claims.

*   **Impact:** The work contributes to the growing body of research on efficient LLM inference. CLaSp's dynamic layer skipping mechanism could be adopted by other researchers and practitioners to further improve the efficiency of speculative decoding. Its "training-free" nature makes it particularly attractive, and it has the potential to influence how LLMs are deployed in real-world applications.
*   **Rigorous Rationale:** CLaSp introduces a clever, practical solution to accelerate LLM inference. The method is appealing due to its training-free nature and context-aware dynamic layer skipping strategy. While the paper demonstrates promising results, broader validation across LLM families and thorough analysis of computational overhead are needed to solidify its general applicability.

**Score: 7**

**Rigorous Rationale:** The core contribution of CLaSp is valuable, but incremental. Prior work has touched upon dynamic layer skipping strategies for acceleration and pre-optimisation methods also exist that serve as a basis for the paper. CLaSp distinguishes itself in the context of speculative decoding with a practical and adaptable design; however, thorough validation and more rigorous comparison are necessary for assigning a higher score. As it stands, the current score reflects its practical nature and reasonable performance enhancements.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Intuitionistic Fuzzy Sets for Large Language Model Data Annotation: A Novel Approach to Side-by-Side Preference Labeling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24199v1)
- **Authors**: Yimin Du
- **Abstract**: The quality of human preference data is crucial for training and evaluating large language models (LLMs), particularly in reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF) and direct preference optimization (DPO) scenarios. Traditional side-by-side (SBS) annotation approaches often struggle with inherent uncertainty, annotator disagreement, and the complexity of preference judgments. This paper introduces a novel framework based on intuitionistic fuzzy sets (IFS) for modeling and aggregating human preferences in LLM data annotation tasks. Our approach captures not only the degree of preference but also the uncertainty and hesitation inherent in human judgment through membership, non-membership, and hesitation degrees. We propose an IFS-based annotation protocol that enables more nuanced preference modeling, develops aggregation methods for handling annotator disagreement, and introduces quality metrics for preference data assessment. Experimental validation on multiple datasets demonstrates that our IFS-based approach significantly improves annotation consistency, reduces annotator fatigue, and produces higher-quality preference data compared to traditional binary and Likert-scale methods. The resulting preference datasets lead to improved model performance in downstream tasks, with 12.3\% improvement in win-rate against baseline models and 15.7\% reduction in annotation time. Our framework provides a principled approach to handling uncertainty in human preference annotation and offers practical benefits for large-scale LLM training.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework leveraging Intuitionistic Fuzzy Sets (IFS) for annotating large language model (LLM) preference data in side-by-side comparison tasks.  The authors argue that traditional annotation methods (binary choices, Likert scales) fail to adequately capture the uncertainty, disagreement, and complexity inherent in human preference judgments. The IFS framework allows annotators to express their preferences using membership (support), non-membership (opposition), and hesitation (uncertainty) degrees.  The paper proposes an IFS-based annotation protocol, aggregation methods for handling annotator disagreement, and quality metrics for preference data assessment.  Experiments on multiple datasets demonstrate that the IFS approach improves annotation consistency, reduces annotator fatigue, and leads to better downstream model performance compared to traditional methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The use of Intuitionistic Fuzzy Sets (IFS) to model human preferences in LLM data annotation *is* a novel idea.  While IFS has been used in other NLP areas, its application to preference annotation for LLMs is, as the authors claim, a contribution.  The specific annotation protocol and aggregation methods tailored to IFS in this context are also novel. However, the novelty is *somewhat* incremental. The underlying mathematical framework of IFS is well-established; the key novelty lies in its application and adaptation to this specific problem.

*   **Significance:** The problem addressed is highly significant. High-quality preference data is crucial for training and aligning LLMs, especially with RLHF and DPO. If the proposed approach genuinely improves annotation quality and downstream model performance, it could have a significant impact on how LLMs are trained.  The documented improvements in win-rate are noteworthy. The demonstrated reduction in annotation time, if reliably repeatable, is also beneficial.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Rigorous Methodology:** The paper presents a clear and well-defined methodology, including the annotation protocol, aggregation methods, and quality metrics.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experimental evaluation is relatively thorough, covering multiple datasets and comparing against standard baselines (binary, Likert).
    *   **Practical Implementation:** The authors discuss practical implementation considerations and provide a complete implementation framework, which enhances the reproducibility and impact of the work.
    *   **Addresses a Real Problem:** LLM alignment through preference learning is a pressing challenge, and the paper tackles a critical bottleneck (data quality) in this process.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** As mentioned earlier, the novelty is somewhat incremental, stemming more from application than groundbreaking theoretical advancements.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Depth:** While the paper provides a good overview of IFS, it could benefit from a more in-depth theoretical analysis of why IFS is particularly well-suited for preference modeling compared to other fuzzy or probabilistic approaches. A more formal justification of the design choices (e.g., the specific aggregation operators) would be beneficial.
    *   **Generalizability Concerns:** While experiments cover multiple datasets, the types of tasks are still relatively narrow (mostly related to helpfulness and safety).
