    *   The "feature knockout" analysis is correlational, and does not establish causation.
    *   Some findings, like the first-position bias in GPT-2, are already well-established.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in several ways:
    *   It provides a benchmark for evaluating the interpretability of SSM-based language models.
    *   It motivates the development of more advanced interpretability techniques for SSMs.
    *   It can inform the design of more efficient and interpretable language model architectures.
    *   The concept of evaluating "context dependence" of Mamba features might find broader utility.

Overall, the paper provides a valuable contribution to the field by extending established interpretability techniques to novel architectures. It provides a framework that might find further utility in more complex analysis. However, the reliance on existing methods and the somewhat limited scope of the analysis prevent it from being a truly transformative contribution.

### LTM3D: Bridging Token Spaces for Conditional 3D Generation with Auto-Regressive Diffusion Framework
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24245v1)
- **Authors**: Xin Kang, Zihan Zheng, Lei Chu, Yue Gao, Jiahao Li, Hao Pan, Xuejin Chen, Yan Lu
- **Abstract**: We present LTM3D, a Latent Token space Modeling framework for conditional 3D shape generation that integrates the strengths of diffusion and auto-regressive (AR) models. While diffusion-based methods effectively model continuous latent spaces and AR models excel at capturing inter-token dependencies, combining these paradigms for 3D shape generation remains a challenge. To address this, LTM3D features a Conditional Distribution Modeling backbone, leveraging a masked autoencoder and a diffusion model to enhance token dependency learning. Additionally, we introduce Prefix Learning, which aligns condition tokens with shape latent tokens during generation, improving flexibility across modalities. We further propose a Latent Token Reconstruction module with Reconstruction-Guided Sampling to reduce uncertainty and enhance structural fidelity in generated shapes. Our approach operates in token space, enabling support for multiple 3D representations, including signed distance fields, point clouds, meshes, and 3D Gaussian Splatting. Extensive experiments on image- and text-conditioned shape generation tasks demonstrate that LTM3D outperforms existing methods in prompt fidelity and structural accuracy while offering a generalizable framework for multi-modal, multi-representation 3D generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the LTM3D paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LTM3D, a framework for conditional 3D shape generation that leverages both diffusion and auto-regressive (AR) models within a latent token space.  LTM3D aims to address the limitations of each individual approach: diffusion models struggle with long sequences and fine-grained details, while AR models are constrained by quantized latent spaces.  The framework features a Conditional Distribution Modeling backbone, a Prefix Learning module to align condition tokens with shape latent tokens, and a Latent Token Reconstruction module with Reconstruction-Guided Sampling to reduce uncertainty and enhance structural fidelity.  LTM3D supports multiple 3D representations (SDF, point clouds, meshes, 3D Gaussians) and demonstrates its effectiveness through image- and text-conditioned shape generation tasks, outperforming existing methods in prompt fidelity and structural accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core novelty lies in the *integration* of diffusion and AR models within a latent token space for 3D shape generation, along with the Prefix Learning and Latent Token Reconstruction modules. Prior work has explored diffusion or AR approaches independently, and some have tackled conditional generation. The specific combination, however, appears to be a relatively unique contribution. The Prefix Learning module provides a novel way to bridge the domain gap between condition and shape tokens. The Latent Token Reconstruction module, with its guided sampling, provides a mechanism to improve sample quality that isn't commonly seen in other 3D generation methods.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from several aspects:

    *   **Multi-modal and multi-representation capability:** The framework's ability to handle multiple 3D data representations *within a single framework* is a definite strength. This reduces the need for separate generation backbones for each data type. The ability to handle both image and text conditions also contributes to its utility.

    *   **Improved fidelity and structural accuracy:** The paper presents quantitative and qualitative evidence that LTM3D produces higher fidelity and structurally more accurate 3D shapes than existing methods, which translates to better practical results.
    *   **Generalizability:** The token space approach potentially lends itself to other modalities and generative tasks beyond 3D shape generation, hinting at a broader impact.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive framework:** The paper presents a well-defined framework with clear explanations of each component.
    *   **Solid experimental validation:** The experimental results on ShapeNet and Objaverse are compelling and demonstrate the advantages of LTM3D over existing methods.  Ablation studies provide insights into the importance of each module.
    *   **Clear writing and presentation:** The paper is generally well-written and easy to follow.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental improvements:** While the individual components are combined in a novel way, they are largely based on existing techniques. This makes the improvements incremental, rather than a completely radical shift.
    *   **Limited ablation depth:** While the ablation studies cover Prefix Learning and Reconstruction Guided Sampling, a deeper exploration of the impact of specific design choices within those modules would strengthen the paper.
    *  **Separate Encoder Training:** The paper highlights the need for a unified latent space, which is an area for future work. 

*   **Potential influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:

    *   Providing a new direction for combining diffusion and AR models for 3D generation.
    *   Establishing a strong baseline for multi-modal, multi-representation 3D shape generation.
    *   Inspiring further research on aligning latent spaces across different 3D representations.
    *   Encouraging the development of more effective tokenization and encoding strategies for 3D data.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

LTM3D presents a valuable contribution to the field of 3D shape generation by effectively integrating diffusion and AR models, incorporating a novel Prefix Learning module, and supporting multiple 3D representations. The demonstrated improvements in fidelity and structural accuracy are significant. However, the reliance on existing techniques and the incremental nature of the advancements temper the overall impact. The limitations regarding training the encoders separately highlight the need for future work on unified latent spaces. The paper's strengths in presenting a comprehensive framework, strong experimental results, and clear writing outweigh the weaknesses, making it a valuable contribution with potential influence.

### Proactive Guidance of Multi-Turn Conversation in Industrial Search
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24251v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaoyu Li, Xiao Li, Li Gao, Yiding Liu, Xiaoyang Wang, Shuaiqiang Wang, Junfeng Wang, Dawei Yin
- **Abstract**: The evolution of Large Language Models (LLMs) has significantly advanced multi-turn conversation systems, emphasizing the need for proactive guidance to enhance users' interactions. However, these systems face challenges in dynamically adapting to shifts in users' goals and maintaining low latency for real-time interactions. In the Baidu Search AI assistant, an industrial-scale multi-turn search system, we propose a novel two-phase framework to provide proactive guidance. The first phase, Goal-adaptive Supervised Fine-Tuning (G-SFT), employs a goal adaptation agent that dynamically adapts to user goal shifts and provides goal-relevant contextual information. G-SFT also incorporates scalable knowledge transfer to distill insights from LLMs into a lightweight model for real-time interaction. The second phase, Click-oriented Reinforcement Learning (C-RL), adopts a generate-rank paradigm, systematically constructs preference pairs from user click signals, and proactively improves click-through rates through more engaging guidance. This dual-phase architecture achieves complementary objectives: G-SFT ensures accurate goal tracking, while C-RL optimizes interaction quality through click signal-driven reinforcement learning. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our framework achieves 86.10% accuracy in offline evaluation (+23.95% over baseline) and 25.28% CTR in online deployment (149.06% relative improvement), while reducing inference latency by 69.55% through scalable knowledge distillation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a two-phase framework for proactive guidance in multi-turn conversation systems within an industrial search context (Baidu Search AI assistant). The framework addresses the challenges of dynamically adapting to shifting user goals and maintaining low latency. The first phase, Goal-adaptive Supervised Fine-Tuning (G-SFT), employs a Goal Adaptation Agent (GAA) to identify and adapt to goal shifts using explicit goal analysis, shift detection signals, and goal-relevant summaries, paired with scalable knowledge transfer (distilling insights from large language models (LLMs) into a smaller, faster model). The second phase, Click-oriented Reinforcement Learning (C-RL), utilizes a generate-rank paradigm to optimize the G-SFT model based on user click signals, improving click-through rates.  Experiments demonstrate improvements in accuracy, click-through rates, and reduced inference latency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Practical Relevance:**  The paper addresses a critical challenge in real-world industrial-scale conversational AI systems: providing relevant and timely guidance in multi-turn interactions. The Baidu Search AI assistant setting lends strong real-world applicability.
    *   **Technical Innovation:** The two-phase approach combining G-SFT and C-RL is a novel architecture. The Goal Adaptation Agent's (GAA) approach to explicitly modeling and adapting to user goal shifts is a significant contribution, particularly the explicit modeling aspect. The generate-rank paradigm for click-oriented reinforcement learning is also a valuable contribution, addressing the challenge of learning from single-click preferences in a multi-output setting.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The empirical results are compelling, showing substantial improvements in accuracy, click-through rate, and latency. The ablation studies clearly demonstrate the effectiveness of each component of the framework (GAA, Scalable Knowledge Transfer, C-RL, and DBS decoding).
    *   **Scalability:** A key focus of the paper is addressing the issue of latency to allow its deployment in an industrial setting. This focus sets it apart from other more academic approaches. The scalable knowledge transfer approach and the efficient GAA are crucial for achieving this scalability.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Baseline Comparison:** While the ERNIE Speed baseline provides a reasonable comparison, it would be more compelling to compare against other state-of-the-art multi-turn dialogue systems and proactive guidance methods, even if those systems do not necessarily focus on the same scale.
    *   **Dataset Specificity:** The dataset is derived from the Baidu Search AI assistant, which provides ecological validity but may limit the generalizability of the findings.  It is difficult to determine how the system would perform in different domains or with different user populations.
    *   **Lack of Theoretical Depth:** The paper focuses primarily on the practical implementation and evaluation of the framework, with relatively little theoretical analysis of the underlying principles or guarantees of its effectiveness.

*   **Novelty:** The combination of goal adaptation, scalable knowledge transfer, and click-oriented reinforcement learning within a two-phase framework is indeed novel. The GAA is a core novelty. The generate-rank paradigm contributes to overcoming the inherent issues with only single user click data.

*   **Significance:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the development of practical and efficient proactive guidance systems for conversational AI, especially in industrial settings. Addressing scalability is key to making these models useful.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution by addressing the critical need for proactive guidance in real-world multi-turn conversation systems. The proposed framework demonstrates strong empirical results and focuses on practical considerations such as scalability and latency. While a wider range of baseline comparisons and more in-depth theoretical analysis would strengthen the paper, the practical relevance and technical innovations outweigh these limitations. It addresses a critical and timely problem with a novel, scalable approach that demonstrates measurable improvements in a real-world setting.

### Interactive Video Generation via Domain Adaptation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24253v1)
- **Authors**: Ishaan Rawal, Suryansh Kumar
- **Abstract**: Text-conditioned diffusion models have emerged as powerful tools for high-quality video generation. However, enabling Interactive Video Generation (IVG), where users control motion elements such as object trajectory, remains challenging. Recent training-free approaches introduce attention masking to guide trajectory, but this often degrades perceptual quality. We identify two key failure modes in these methods, both of which we interpret as domain shift problems, and propose solutions inspired by domain adaptation. First, we attribute the perceptual degradation to internal covariate shift induced by attention masking, as pretrained models are not trained to handle masked attention. To address this, we propose mask normalization, a pre-normalization layer designed to mitigate this shift via distribution matching. Second, we address initialization gap, where the randomly sampled initial noise does not align with IVG conditioning, by introducing a temporal intrinsic diffusion prior that enforces spatio-temporal consistency at each denoising step. Extensive qualitative and quantitative evaluations demonstrate that mask normalization and temporal intrinsic denoising improve both perceptual quality and trajectory control over the existing state-of-the-art IVG techniques.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of Interactive Video Generation (IVG), where users control object motion (trajectory) in generated videos using text prompts and bounding box trajectories.  Current training-free IVG methods employing attention masking often degrade perceptual quality. The authors identify two key issues: (1) Internal Covariate Shift:  Masking attention layers introduces domain shift because pre-trained diffusion models aren't trained on masked attention. (2) Initialization Gap: The randomly sampled initial noise doesn't align well with the desired IVG conditioning.  To address these problems, they propose: (1) Mask Normalization: A pre-normalization layer that aligns feature distributions between masked and unmasked attention outputs. (2) Temporal Intrinsic Denoising (TID): A modification to the diffusion sampling process, guided by a classifier and a correlative temporal prior, to ensure spatiotemporal consistency during denoising. The authors demonstrate that their method achieves state-of-the-art performance, improving both trajectory control and perceptual quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**
    *   **High-Impact Ideas:**  The paper makes a notable contribution by framing the degradation of perceptual quality in training-free IVG methods as a *domain adaptation* problem. Identifying the internal covariate shift due to attention masking and the initialization gap is a key insight.
    *   **Incremental but Effective Techniques:**  While the individual components (mask normalization, and temporal intrinsic denoising) borrow inspiration from existing domain adaptation techniques (style transfer, deep image prior), the *combination* and *application* to the specific challenges of training-free IVG with attention masking are what make the work novel. The "temporal prior" is a clever way to regularize the denoising process, and the explicit cross-token and cross-frame interactions are useful.
    *   **Pre-Normalization:** The pre-normalization layer helps in aligning feature distributions between the outputs of the masked and unmasked attention layers.
*   **Significance:**
    *   **Improved IVG Quality:** The results, both quantitative and qualitative, demonstrate a significant improvement over existing training-free IVG methods. This is a meaningful step towards more usable and controllable video generation tools. The consistent performance across metrics (CLIP-SIM, Coverage, mIoU, FID, KID) is a strong point.
    *   **Training-Free Approach:**  The training-free aspect is critical.  It makes the method highly practical and readily adaptable to new pre-trained video diffusion models. This enhances its accessibility and broadens its applicability.
    *   **Addressing a Bottleneck:**  The paper directly addresses a known bottleneck: the tradeoff between trajectory control and perceptual quality in current training-free IVG. Overcoming this tradeoff unlocks new capabilities for interactive video creation.
    *   **Extensive Validation:** The authors present compelling qualitative results and a solid set of quantitative experiments, including ablations, making a strong case for the effectiveness of their proposed methods.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Existing Work:** The approach is heavily built upon Zeroscope and Peekaboo. While adapting and improving on existing work is common and valuable, the degree of dependence slightly reduces the overall impact.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The paper focuses on single-subject, static-camera scenarios. While the authors acknowledge this limitation, it's important to consider that many real-world applications involve more complex scenes. Future research will be required to address multi-agent scenarios and dynamic camera control.
    *   **Unclear Generalizability:** While the authors argue their methods are modality-agnostic, the experiments are limited to a specific diffusion model (Zeroscope). Evaluating on other models would strengthen the claim.
    *   **Limited Failure Cases:** It would be interesting to analyze the effect of using different frozen steps.
*   **Impact:**

    The paper's focus on improving the quality and controllability of IVG through domain adaptation could have a significant influence on the field. The identified limitations of attention masking in the training-free setting and the solutions proposed provide a valuable framework for future research. The simplicity and effectiveness of the proposed techniques increase the likelihood of adoption.

**Justification:**

The paper presents a well-defined problem, thoroughly analyzes existing limitations, and proposes effective and practical solutions grounded in established domain adaptation techniques. The experimental results are compelling, and the paper is generally well-written. However, the significant reliance on existing architectures and the limited scope in terms of scene complexity and camera control restrain the overall novelty and impact.

### Effects of Theory of Mind and Prosocial Beliefs on Steering Human-Aligned Behaviors of LLMs in Ultimatum Games
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24255v1)
- **Authors**: Neemesh Yadav, Palakorn Achananuparp, Jing Jiang, Ee-Peng Lim
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown potential in simulating human behaviors and performing theory-of-mind (ToM) reasoning, a crucial skill for complex social interactions. In this study, we investigate the role of ToM reasoning in aligning agentic behaviors with human norms in negotiation tasks, using the ultimatum game as a controlled environment. We initialized LLM agents with different prosocial beliefs (including Greedy, Fair, and Selfless) and reasoning methods like chain-of-thought (CoT) and varying ToM levels, and examined their decision-making processes across diverse LLMs, including reasoning models like o3-mini and DeepSeek-R1 Distilled Qwen 32B. Results from 2,700 simulations indicated that ToM reasoning enhances behavior alignment, decision-making consistency, and negotiation outcomes. Consistent with previous findings, reasoning models exhibit limited capability compared to models with ToM reasoning, different roles of the game benefits with different orders of ToM reasoning. Our findings contribute to the understanding of ToM's role in enhancing human-AI interaction and cooperative decision-making. The code used for our experiments can be found at https://github.com/Stealth-py/UltimatumToM.
- **Summary**: Okay, I can provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper based on the provided text.

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the effects of Theory of Mind (ToM) reasoning and prosocial beliefs on aligning the behavior of Large Language Models (LLMs) with human norms in the ultimatum game. The authors initialized LLM agents with different prosocial beliefs (Greedy, Fair, Selfless) and reasoning methods (Chain-of-Thought, varying ToM levels). They then analyzed the decision-making processes of these agents using various LLMs, including 03-mini and DeepSeek-R1 Distilled Qwen 32B, across a series of 2,700 simulations. The results indicate that incorporating ToM reasoning enhances behavior alignment, decision-making consistency, and negotiation outcomes. Further, the study compared different reasoning methods, exploring their impact on LLM negotiation and belief combinations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clearly Defined Research Question:** The paper tackles a relevant research question concerning the behavioral alignment of LLMs in social simulations, a critical area as LLMs are increasingly deployed in interactive environments. The study explicitly addresses whether ToM reasoning steers agent behavior to be more closely aligned with human behavior.
    *   **Comprehensive Experimental Setup:** The use of the ultimatum game as a controlled environment is a good choice, allowing for structured investigation. The experimental design is also relatively comprehensive, considering multiple LLMs, a variety of prosocial beliefs, and different reasoning methods.  The inclusion of 2700 simulations provides a good basis for statistical analysis.
    *   **Detailed Analysis and Metrics:** The authors employ a variety of evaluation metrics, including acceptance rate, average turns, and deviation scores, which provide a multi-faceted view of the agent's behavior. They also perform statistical analyses (OLS regressions) to assess the impact of different factors on these metrics.
    *   **Consideration of Limitations:** The authors acknowledge limitations in the study, such as the use of the ultimatum game which might limit real-world generalizability, the sensitivity of results to prompt engineering, and the lack of emotional or social dynamics considerations.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Novelty:** The paper builds upon existing work on agent-based simulations of economic games, and the application of ToM reasoning to LLMs. The incremental contribution might be small. While the multi-level ToM framework is inspired by existing models, the core concept isn't groundbreaking.  The idea that ToM improves human alignment isn't a totally novel idea.
    *   **Heuristic Expected Behavior:** The reliance on heuristically defined ranges of expected human behavior, while motivated by prior research, may introduce biases. The absence of parallel experimental data from human participants limits a truly direct comparison with human behavior.  A stronger evaluation could have involved human subject data for a clearer benchmark.
    *   **Limited Depth in BDI Analysis:** The claim that the study follows the classic Belief-Desire-Intention (BDI) model is superficial. While they incorporate beliefs, the operationalization of desires and intentions appears simplified and lacks the nuance of traditional BDI models.
    *   **Findings are Incremental:** The overall impact of the findings might be somewhat incremental. The analysis focuses on which factors enhance alignment, which is helpful, but it doesn't revolutionize our understanding of LLM behavior or ToM.
    *   **Lack of Detail in Results:** The tables are very dense and while there is quite a bit of data, it takes work to parse out meaningful insight. It can be argued the findings are not particularly shocking in any way.

*   **Significance:**
    *   The paper contributes to a better understanding of how to design and deploy LLMs that interact more effectively and cooperatively with humans in negotiation tasks. This is becoming increasingly important as LLMs are used in diverse domains such as customer service, healthcare, and education.
    *   The study highlights the importance of considering both ToM reasoning and prosocial beliefs when designing LLM agents, as these factors can significantly influence their behavior and outcomes.
    *   The findings provide practical guidance for developers on how to improve the alignment of LLM agents with human norms, which is crucial for building trust and ensuring ethical AI systems.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a well-executed study with a clear research question, a comprehensive experimental setup, and a thorough analysis. However, its novelty is limited, and the incremental nature of the findings suggests that the contribution to the field might be smaller than it appears on the surface. The limitations of the human behavioral baseline further reduce the significance. Therefore, based on the strengths and weaknesses above, a score of 6 is most fitting.

### FABLE: A Novel Data-Flow Analysis Benchmark on Procedural Text for Large Language Model Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24258v1)
- **Authors**: Vishal Pallagani, Nitin Gupta, John Aydin, Biplav Srivastava
- **Abstract**: Understanding how data moves, transforms, and persists, known as data flow, is fundamental to reasoning in procedural tasks. Despite their fluency in natural and programming languages, large language models (LLMs), although increasingly being applied to decisions with procedural tasks, have not been systematically evaluated for their ability to perform data-flow reasoning. We introduce FABLE, an extensible benchmark designed to assess LLMs' understanding of data flow using structured, procedural text. FABLE adapts eight classical data-flow analyses from software engineering: reaching definitions, very busy expressions, available expressions, live variable analysis, interval analysis, type-state analysis, taint analysis, and concurrency analysis. These analyses are instantiated across three real-world domains: cooking recipes, travel routes, and automated plans. The benchmark includes 2,400 question-answer pairs, with 100 examples for each domain-analysis combination. We evaluate three types of LLMs: a reasoning-focused model (DeepSeek-R1 8B), a general-purpose model (LLaMA 3.1 8B), and a code-specific model (Granite Code 8B). Each model is tested using majority voting over five sampled completions per prompt. Results show that the reasoning model achieves higher accuracy, but at the cost of over 20 times slower inference compared to the other models. In contrast, the general-purpose and code-specific models perform close to random chance. FABLE provides the first diagnostic benchmark to systematically evaluate data-flow reasoning and offers insights for developing models with stronger procedural understanding.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "FABLE: A Novel Data-Flow Analysis Benchmark on Procedural Text for Large Language Model Evaluation."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FABLE, a new benchmark to assess the data-flow reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) when processing procedural texts (recipes, travel routes, automated plans). FABLE adapts eight classical data-flow analyses from software engineering (reaching definitions, available expressions, etc.) and applies them to these procedural text domains. The benchmark consists of 2,400 question-answer pairs.  The authors evaluate three types of LLMs: reasoning-focused, general-purpose, and code-specific models.  The results demonstrate that while a reasoning-focused model performs better, there's still a significant gap in performance, highlighting the difficulty of data-flow reasoning for current LLMs. General-purpose and code-specific models performed close to chance. FABLE is presented as a diagnostic benchmark to evaluate and improve procedural understanding in LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the **adaptation of well-established data-flow analysis techniques from software engineering to the domain of natural language procedural text.** While procedural text understanding benchmarks exist, they tend to focus on isolated aspects like causality or entity state tracking. FABLE provides a more comprehensive and structured evaluation by leveraging these formal analysis techniques. The combination of these techniques with the chosen domains (recipes, travel routes, plans) offers a unique testbed. However, one could argue that the core techniques themselves aren't new, just their application.  It's a clever transfer of knowledge, but not fundamentally new algorithmic development.

*   **Significance:** The significance is multi-fold:

    *   **Diagnostic Capability:** FABLE fills a gap by providing a *diagnostic* benchmark.  It allows researchers to pinpoint specific areas where LLMs struggle with procedural understanding, rather than just getting a high-level accuracy score. The eight data-flow analyses act as targeted probes.
    *   **Real-World Relevance:** Procedural texts are ubiquitous.  Improving LLMs' ability to reason about them has broad implications for applications like recipe generation, autonomous navigation, and robotic task planning. The chosen domains are indeed representative and relevant.
    *   **Structured Evaluation:** The paper provides a structured and rigorous evaluation methodology. By using techniques from SE, it can enable easier comparisons of different LLMs' reasoning capabilities.
    *   **Insights for Future Research:** The empirical results reveal clear performance limitations of current LLMs, particularly the vast difference between the accuracy of the reasoning model (deepseek-r1:8b) and the general-purpose/code-specific models. It motivates future research into architectures and training methods that can better handle data-flow reasoning in natural language. The paper rightly suggests hybrid neurosymbolic methods, structured fine-tuning, and targeted pretraining as future directions.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the problem of data-flow reasoning in procedural text and motivates its importance.
    *   **Well-Designed Benchmark:** The FABLE benchmark is carefully designed, with a diverse set of analyses and domains. The data generation and curation process is described in detail, promoting reproducibility.
    *   **Thorough Evaluation:** The authors conduct a thorough empirical evaluation, comparing different LLMs and analyzing their performance across various dimensions.
    *   **Reproducibility:** Data and code are provided on github.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Analyses:** While eight data-flow analyses are included, there are others that could be relevant. Also, the benchmark represents only *one* formal interpretation for each analysis. Alternative definitions or relaxed variants may yield different insights, but this aspect has not been explored.
    *   **Prompt Engineering:** The choice of prompt templates could influence the results. A more extensive prompt engineering exploration, including chain-of-thought prompting, would further strengthen the analysis and explore if a better prompt could affect performance of the baseline models.
    *   **Binary Question Format:** The choice of binary question-answer format, while simplifying evaluation, might obscure intermediate reasoning errors.

*   **Potential Influence:** FABLE is likely to become a widely used benchmark in the field of NLP and LLM research. Its diagnostic capabilities and focus on a core reasoning skill make it a valuable tool for researchers working on improving LLMs' understanding of procedural text. It has good potential to drive research in novel AI architectures that can tackle the intricacies of real-world reasoning.

*   **Score Justification:** While the paper does a excellent job adapting software engineering techniques to natural language, and the problem it addresses is important, the core dataflow analysis techniques themselves are not novel. However, its significance in advancing the procedural understanding of LLMs, alongside its diagnostic utility and well-structured framework, it is nonetheless a valuable contribution.

**Score: 7.5** The paper presents a high-quality benchmark with clear practical value and the potential to significantly influence research directions in the field, making it a solid contribution, despite not presenting any fundamentally new AI methods or algorithms.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Generative AI for Urban Design: A Stepwise Approach Integrating Human Expertise with Multimodal Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24260v1)
- **Authors**: Mingyi He, Yuebing Liang, Shenhao Wang, Yunhan Zheng, Qingyi Wang, Dingyi Zhuang, Li Tian, Jinhua Zhao
- **Abstract**: Urban design is a multifaceted process that demands careful consideration of site-specific constraints and collaboration among diverse professionals and stakeholders. The advent of generative artificial intelligence (GenAI) offers transformative potential by improving the efficiency of design generation and facilitating the communication of design ideas. However, most existing approaches are not well integrated with human design workflows. They often follow end-to-end pipelines with limited control, overlooking the iterative nature of real-world design. This study proposes a stepwise generative urban design framework that integrates multimodal diffusion models with human expertise to enable more adaptive and controllable design processes. Instead of generating design outcomes in a single end-to-end process, the framework divides the process into three key stages aligned with established urban design workflows: (1) road network and land use planning, (2) building layout planning, and (3) detailed planning and rendering. At each stage, multimodal diffusion models generate preliminary designs based on textual prompts and image-based constraints, which can then be reviewed and refined by human designers. We design an evaluation framework to assess the fidelity, compliance, and diversity of the generated designs. Experiments using data from Chicago and New York City demonstrate that our framework outperforms baseline models and end-to-end approaches across all three dimensions. This study underscores the benefits of multimodal diffusion models and stepwise generation in preserving human control and facilitating iterative refinements, laying the groundwork for human-AI interaction in urban design solutions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a stepwise generative AI framework for urban design that integrates human expertise with multimodal diffusion models. This framework divides the urban design process into three stages: road network and land use planning, building layout planning, and detailed planning/rendering. At each stage, a ControlNet diffusion model generates preliminary designs based on textual prompts and image-based constraints, allowing human designers to review and refine the designs iteratively. The authors design an evaluation framework focusing on visual fidelity, instruction compliance, and design diversity. They conduct experiments in Chicago and New York City, showing that their stepwise approach outperforms baseline models and end-to-end methods. The paper emphasizes the benefits of this approach in preserving human control, facilitating iterative refinements, and laying the groundwork for human-AI collaboration in urban design.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a gap:** The paper tackles a crucial limitation in current AI-driven urban design, which often lacks sufficient human control and overlooks the iterative nature of real-world design processes. This is a significant and relevant problem.
    *   **Human-in-the-loop approach:** The stepwise framework is a well-structured attempt to integrate human expertise at multiple stages of design, aligning with established urban design workflows. This is a more practical and adoptable approach compared to end-to-end AI solutions.
    *   **Multimodal diffusion models:** Leveraging ControlNet diffusion models allows for incorporating both image-based constraints (site conditions) and textual prompts (planning guidance), enabling flexible and controllable design generation.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The authors develop a detailed evaluation framework to assess visual fidelity, instruction compliance, and design diversity, providing a robust assessment of the framework's performance.
    *   **Empirical validation:** The experiments in Chicago and New York City provide empirical evidence that supports the proposed framework's superiority over baseline models and end-to-end approaches.
    *   **Reproducibility**: The authors provide code availability, promoting open science and reproducibilty.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited dataset diversity:** The study focuses on only two U.S. cities. Expanding the dataset to include a wider variety of cities with diverse urban structures would improve the generalizability of the framework.
    *   **Focus on urban form:** The framework primarily addresses urban form (geometric and visual compliance) but does not delve deeply into the generative logic of urban spaces (socio-economic, environmental, or cultural factors). This represents a limitation in the scope of design guidance.
    *   **Greenfield development bias**: It is mostly suited for greenfield development and does not address urban renewal sufficiently.
    *   **Qualitative Diversity Evaluation**: The evaluation of the design diversity is done qualitatively rather than quantitatively.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The primary novelty lies in the integration of human-in-the-loop concepts into a stepwise AI framework specifically tailored for urban design. While individual components (diffusion models, ControlNet, image-to-image translation) are not entirely new, their combination and application in this context represent a novel contribution.

    *   The significance comes from the potential to bridge the gap between theoretical advancements in AI and practical applications in urban planning. The framework provides a more adoptable and controllable solution for urban designers, which could potentially improve design efficiency, facilitate public engagement, and support decision-making.

    *   However, the limited dataset and the focus on urban form somewhat constrain the scope of the contribution. Further research is needed to address these limitations and expand the framework's applicability.

*   **Potential influence on the field:** The paper could influence the field by encouraging more research into human-AI collaborative approaches for urban design. The emphasis on stepwise design, iterative refinement, and contextual constraints could inspire future frameworks and models.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, and focusing on the novelty and significance, I assign a score of 7.5. The paper makes a valuable contribution to integrating AI and urban design, addressing a key limitation with a well-structured framework and empirical validation. The limitations regarding dataset diversity and the scope of design guidance prevent a higher score.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Simulating Training Data Leakage in Multiple-Choice Benchmarks for LLM Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24263v1)
- **Authors**: Naila Shafirni Hidayat, Muhammad Dehan Al Kautsar, Alfan Farizki Wicaksono, Fajri Koto
- **Abstract**: The performance of large language models (LLMs) continues to improve, as reflected in rising scores on standard benchmarks. However, the lack of transparency around training data raises concerns about potential overlap with evaluation sets and the fairness of reported results. Although prior work has proposed methods for detecting data leakage, these approaches primarily focus on identifying outliers and have not been evaluated under controlled simulated leakage conditions. In this work, we compare existing leakage detection techniques, namely permutation and n-gram-based methods, under a continual pretraining setup that simulates real-world leakage scenarios, and additionally explore a lightweight method we call semi-half question. Although semi-half offers a low-cost alternative, our analysis shows that the n-gram method consistently achieves the highest F1-score. We also refine these techniques to support instance-level detection and reduce computational overhead. Leveraging the best-performing method, we create cleaned versions of MMLU and HellaSwag, and re-evaluate several LLMs. Our findings present a practical path toward more reliable and transparent evaluations, and we recommend contamination checks as a standard step before releasing benchmark results.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the growing concern of data contamination in large language model (LLM) evaluation benchmarks. It investigates the effectiveness of existing and novel leakage detection methods under controlled, simulated conditions, focusing on multiple-choice question (MCQ) benchmarks like MMLU and HellaSwag. The authors simulate training data leakage using continual pre-training. They compare three leakage detection methods: a lightweight "semi-half" question method, the permutation method (and two improved variants, permutation-R and permutation-Q), and the n-gram method. The paper finds that the n-gram method consistently achieves the highest F1-score and refines this method for instance-level detection. Leveraging the best-performing method, they create cleaned versions of MMLU and HellaSwag and re-evaluate several LLMs, demonstrating shifts in performance rankings after removing potential leakage. The paper advocates for contamination checks as a standard practice before releasing benchmark results.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** While the problem of data contamination is well-known, the paper offers a valuable contribution by providing a **controlled comparative analysis** of different detection methods under simulated leakage conditions, rather than just focusing on outlier detection as many prior works do. The introduction of the `permutation-Q` method, offering a computationally cheaper quadratic approximation compared to the factorial complexity of other permutations is a plus. The refinement of the n-gram method for instance-level detection is also a worthy improvement.

*   **Significance:** The paper provides practical guidance for researchers evaluating LLMs. The analysis and cleaned datasets address a critical reliability issue in benchmarking. Identifying the n-gram method as a consistent performer, especially its adaptation to instance-level detection, gives a clear path forward for practitioners. Re-evaluating LLMs on the cleaned datasets and observing shifts in performance rankings directly demonstrates the impact of data contamination on reported results. The advocacy for contamination checks as a standard step provides a strong recommendation for the community.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Controlled Experiments:** The simulated leakage setup allows for a more rigorous assessment than simply looking for outliers.
    *   **Comparative Analysis:** Directly comparing different detection methods is valuable for understanding their relative strengths and weaknesses.
    *   **Practical Recommendations:** The paper offers concrete advice on which detection methods to use and provides cleaned datasets.
    *   **Performance Shift Demonstration:** Showing how performance changes after removing potential leakage strengthens the argument for routine contamination checks.
    *   **Improved efficiency:** Improved on the efficiency of existing methods by reducing computational overheads (permutation-R and permutation-Q)

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Leakage Simulation Limitations:** Continual pre-training, while providing controlled simulation, may not perfectly reflect real-world data contamination scenarios, which can be more subtle and complex.  Real-world LLMs are trained on diverse datasets, not just repetitions of the benchmark data.
    *   **Limited Scope of Datasets and Models:** The study focuses on MMLU and HellaSwag and only evaluates a limited set of models. While these are popular benchmarks, the findings might not generalize to all LLM evaluation tasks.
    *   **Dependence on N-Gram Threshold:** The n-gram method's performance is sensitive to the chosen threshold. While the authors tune this parameter, the optimal threshold may vary depending on the specific dataset and models being evaluated.
    *  **False Positives:** High false positive on Moral Scenarios requires that this is addressed in a future iteration.

*   **Impact:** This paper has the potential to influence how LLMs are evaluated, encouraging researchers to adopt more rigorous contamination checks. The cleaned datasets can serve as a valuable resource for future benchmarking efforts. The analysis of different detection methods can guide researchers in choosing the most appropriate methods for their specific needs.

**Justification of Score:**

I assign a score of **7** to this paper. It provides a valuable, comparative analysis of data contamination detection methods for LLMs. The controlled experiments, practical recommendations, and demonstration of performance shifts contribute to the field. The limitations related to the scope of datasets, and  sensitivity of the methods keep the score from being higher. The innovation in reducing the complexity of the permutation method strengthens the work. Overall, the paper addresses a critical issue in LLM evaluation and offers actionable guidance for the community.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Faithful and Robust LLM-Driven Theorem Proving for NLI Explanations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24264v1)
- **Authors**: Xin Quan, Marco Valentino, Louise A. Dennis, André Freitas
- **Abstract**: Natural language explanations play a fundamental role in Natural Language Inference (NLI) by revealing how premises logically entail hypotheses. Recent work has shown that the interaction of large language models (LLMs) with theorem provers (TPs) can help verify and improve the validity of NLI explanations. However, TPs require translating natural language into machine-verifiable formal representations, a process that introduces the risk of semantic information loss and unfaithful interpretation, an issue compounded by LLMs' challenges in capturing critical logical structures with sufficient precision. Moreover, LLMs are still limited in their capacity for rigorous and robust proof construction within formal verification frameworks. To mitigate issues related to faithfulness and robustness, this paper investigates strategies to (1) alleviate semantic loss during autoformalisation, (2) efficiently identify and correct syntactic errors in logical representations, (3) explicitly use logical expressions to guide LLMs in generating structured proof sketches, and (4) increase LLMs' capacity of interpreting TP's feedback for iterative refinement. Our empirical results on e-SNLI, QASC and WorldTree using different LLMs demonstrate that the proposed strategies yield significant improvements in autoformalisation (+18.46%, +34.2%, +39.77%) and explanation refinement (+29.5%, +51.5%, +41.25%) over the state-of-the-art model. Moreover, we show that specific interventions on the hybrid LLM-TP architecture can substantially improve efficiency, drastically reducing the number of iterations required for successful verification.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Faithful and Robust LLM-Driven Theorem Proving for NLI Explanations":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of generating logically sound and faithful natural language explanations for Natural Language Inference (NLI) using Large Language Models (LLMs) and theorem provers (TPs). It focuses on mitigating common issues in neuro-symbolic approaches, such as semantic loss during autoformalization (converting natural language to formal logic), syntactic errors in logical representations, ineffective proof construction by LLMs, and limited LLM capacity to interpret TP feedback.

The authors propose a framework called Faithful-Refiner. Key strategies include: 1) using syntactic parsing to guide autoformalization; 2) implementing quantifier and logical consistency checks with refinement processes; 3) extracting logical propositions/relations to guide proof sketch generation; and 4) iteratively refining explanations based on TP feedback. The framework builds upon the previous Explanation-Refiner work. The authors test their approach on e-SNLI, QASC, and WorldTree datasets using GPT-4o, GPT-4o-mini, Llama3.1-70b, and DeepSeek-V3 demonstrating improved autoformalization and explanation refinement compared to the state-of-the-art. They also conduct ablation studies to show the importance of each component.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a Significant Problem:** The paper tackles a key challenge in explainable AI (XAI) – generating logically valid explanations for NLI. The faithfulness and robustness of these explanations are crucial for building trust and reliability in AI systems.
*   **Comprehensive Approach:** The proposed framework addresses multiple facets of the LLM-TP integration problem, from autoformalization fidelity to proof refinement strategies. This holistic approach is valuable.
*   **Clearly Defined Strategies:** The paper clearly outlines specific techniques for each stage of the pipeline (syntactic parsing guidance, quantifier checks, proof sketch generation, etc.), making the method understandable and potentially reproducible.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The quantitative evaluation on multiple datasets and with different LLMs provides convincing evidence of the framework's effectiveness. The improvements over the previous state-of-the-art are substantial in several cases.
*   **Ablation Studies:**  These demonstrate the importance of different components and give insight into their contributions.
*   **Code Availability:** The authors make their code and data available, promoting reproducibility.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** The framework builds heavily on previous work (Explanation-Refiner). While the individual techniques are valuable additions, the overall novelty is arguably somewhat limited. A detailed comparison section to the previous work could have been presented to emphasize on the novelties of the work.
*   **Dependence on LLMs:** The approach is inherently limited by the capabilities and biases of the underlying LLMs. While the paper uses multiple LLMs, the fundamental limitations of LLMs (hallucinations, biases) still apply, potentially affecting the quality of explanations. More discussion of how to address these LLM limitations is needed.
*   **Complexity and Scalability:** The integration of various components (syntactic parsing, theorem proving, logical extraction) adds complexity to the system.  While performance is good, scalability to larger or more complex NLI tasks is not thoroughly addressed.
*   **Lack of Human Evaluation of Explanations:** The focus is on automatic metrics (autoformalization faithfulness, refinement rates). While necessary, including human evaluations of the generated explanations' understandability and usefulness would strengthen the paper. Do people find these refined explanations *better*?
*   **Limited Discussion of Limitations:** While limitations are acknowledged, a more in-depth discussion of the edge cases and scenarios where the framework struggles would be beneficial.

**Significance:**

The paper contributes to the growing body of work on neuro-symbolic AI for NLI explanation. By focusing on faithfulness and robustness, it takes an important step toward building reliable and trustworthy XAI systems. The proposed techniques for mitigating LLM limitations and improving TP integration are valuable contributions that could influence future research in the field.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper offers valuable improvements and demonstrates strong results, the incremental nature of the novelty and the inherent reliance on potentially flawed LLMs temper its overall impact.  The lack of human evaluation is a notable omission. The paper addresses an important problem and presents a well-executed solution but does not represent a groundbreaking advance.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### MUSE: Model-Agnostic Tabular Watermarking via Multi-Sample Selection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24267v1)
- **Authors**: Liancheng Fang, Aiwei Liu, Henry Peng Zou, Yankai Chen, Hengrui Zhang, Zhongfen Deng, Philip S. Yu
- **Abstract**: We introduce MUSE, a watermarking algorithm for tabular generative models. Previous approaches typically leverage DDIM invertibility to watermark tabular diffusion models, but tabular diffusion models exhibit significantly poorer invertibility compared to other modalities, compromising performance. Simultaneously, tabular diffusion models require substantially less computation than other modalities, enabling a multi-sample selection approach to tabular generative model watermarking. MUSE embeds watermarks by generating multiple candidate samples and selecting one based on a specialized scoring function, without relying on model invertibility. Our theoretical analysis establishes the relationship between watermark detectability, candidate count, and dataset size, allowing precise calibration of watermarking strength. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MUSE achieves state-of-the-art watermark detectability and robustness against various attacks while maintaining data quality, and remains compatible with any tabular generative model supporting repeated sampling, effectively addressing key challenges in tabular data watermarking. Specifically, it reduces the distortion rates on fidelity metrics by 81-89%, while achieving a 1.0 TPR@0.1%FPR detection rate. Implementation of MUSE can be found at https://github.com/fangliancheng/MUSE.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MUSE, a model-agnostic watermarking technique for tabular generative models. Unlike previous approaches that rely on the invertibility of tabular diffusion models (often compromised due to lower invertibility compared to image/video domains), MUSE adopts a multi-sample selection approach. It generates multiple candidate samples for each data row and selects one based on a specialized scoring function (using a secret watermark key), without relying on model inversion. The paper provides theoretical analysis relating watermark detectability to candidate count and dataset size, enabling watermarking strength calibration. Experiments demonstrate MUSE's state-of-the-art detectability and robustness against various attacks, while maintaining data quality and model compatibility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the *model-agnostic* nature of the watermarking technique. Existing generative watermarking approaches for tabular data, such as TabWak, heavily depend on the invertibility of specific diffusion models. This paper successfully bypasses this limitation by leveraging a multi-sample selection approach. The scoring function based on statistical properties of the data row to choose columns is a good insight. However, the fundamental idea of multi-sample selection, in isolation, is not entirely new in the context of generative models; the novelty is in applying and adapting it effectively to tabular data watermarking to circumvent the invertibility problem. The theoretical analysis providing a direct relationship between number of sample, data size, and watermark detectability is also an important contribution.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work is potentially high. Tabular data is widely used and increasingly generated synthetically, making ownership verification and misuse detection critical. MUSE's model-agnostic property makes it applicable to a broader range of models. The increased robustness against attacks, especially those targeting tabular data, is valuable. The reduction in distortion compared to inversion-based techniques is another important factor contributing to the significance of this work. Moreover, The paper provides a valuable alternative, which can encourage further research into model-agnostic watermarking techniques.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Model-agnostic: Not tied to a specific generative model architecture.
    *   Good performance: High detectability, robustness, and data quality preservation.
    *   Sound Theoretical Analysis: Provides calibration mechanism.
    *   Clear Experiments: Well-designed experiments with strong baselines.
    *   Practical: Potentially easier to implement and use than inversion-based methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Multi-Sample Overhead: The multi-sample generation increases computation compared to a single-sample generation with inversion.  While the paper argues for lower computation per sample generation in tabular models, the total GFLOPs may be higher due to repeating the sampling process. The experiments do demonstrate faster runtime than inversion based approaches.
    *   Column Deletion Vulnerability: The watermark detectability drops under column deletion attacks, because the watermark is embedded via selected columns, which are partially removed by this attack.
    *   Limited Scope of Theoretical Analysis: While the theoretical analysis is valuable, it is based on certain assumption regarding the scoring function and random variables. Further analysis under weaker assumptions could enhance the analysis.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in tabular data watermarking by establishing a new direction that avoids invertibility issues. It could also be used in practice to protect tabular data generated by various models.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and effective solution to a significant problem. It successfully addresses the limitations of previous methods by introducing a model-agnostic approach with strong empirical performance. The theoretical analysis further strengthens the contribution. While it has some limitations, they do not significantly detract from the overall value. For these reasons, a score of 8 is justified.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### How Much Backtracking is Enough? Exploring the Interplay of SFT and RL in Enhancing LLM Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24273v1)
- **Authors**: Hongyi James Cai, Junlin Wang, Xiaoyin Chen, Bhuwan Dhingra
- **Abstract**: Recent breakthroughs in large language models (LLMs) have effectively improved their reasoning abilities, particularly on mathematical and logical problems that have verifiable answers, through techniques such as supervised finetuning (SFT) and reinforcement learning (RL). Prior research indicates that RL effectively internalizes search strategies, enabling long chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning, with backtracking emerging naturally as a learned capability. However, the precise benefits of backtracking, specifically, how significantly it contributes to reasoning improvements and the optimal extent of its use, remain poorly understood. In this work, we systematically investigate the dynamics between SFT and RL on eight reasoning tasks: Countdown, Sudoku, Arc 1D, Geometry, Color Cube Rotation, List Functions, Zebra Puzzles, and Self Reference. Our findings highlight that short CoT sequences used in SFT as a warm-up do have moderate contribution to RL training, compared with cold-start RL; however such contribution diminishes when tasks become increasingly difficult. Motivated by this observation, we construct synthetic datasets varying systematically in the number of backtracking steps and conduct controlled experiments to isolate the influence of either the correctness (content) or the structure (i.e., backtrack frequency). We find that (1) longer CoT with backtracks generally induce better and more stable RL training, (2) more challenging problems with larger search space tend to need higher numbers of backtracks during the SFT stage. Additionally, we demonstrate through experiments on distilled data that RL training is largely unaffected by the correctness of long CoT sequences, suggesting that RL prioritizes structural patterns over content correctness. Collectively, our results offer practical insights into designing optimal training strategies to effectively scale reasoning in LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the interplay between supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and reinforcement learning (RL) in enhancing the reasoning abilities of large language models (LLMs).  Specifically, it focuses on the impact of "backtracking" - the model's ability to revise its reasoning steps - during the SFT warm-up phase before RL. Through controlled experiments on eight reasoning tasks, the authors explore the necessity and optimal extent of backtracking in SFT to improve subsequent RL training. The key findings include: (1) Short CoT sequences used in SFT warm-ups contribute to RL training compared to cold-start RL; (2) Longer CoT with more backtracks generally lead to better and more stable RL training, but the number of backtracks needed depends on the task difficulty; (3) RL training is relatively unaffected by the correctness of long CoT sequences used for SFT, suggesting that RL prioritizes structural patterns like backtracking over content accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its systematic investigation of the impact of backtracking during the SFT warm-up stage for RL-based reasoning enhancement. Prior work had identified backtracking as a useful behavior, but this paper attempts to dissect its specific contributions, explore the relationship between task difficulty and optimal backtracking frequency, and surprisingly, to downplay the role of correctness during the pre-RL phase. Disentangling the contribution of SFT (warm-up) vs. RL is a valuable undertaking.

**Significance:** The findings offer practical insights for designing effective training strategies for LLMs to improve their reasoning abilities.  Specifically:
*   The discovery that correctness may be secondary to structure (backtracking) in pre-RL SFT is significant. If confirmed by further research on a diverse set of task and models, it could lead to more efficient training pipelines. It suggests that even noisy or partially incorrect reasoning traces can be beneficial for seeding the RL process, which reduces the resources required for curating only high-quality data.

*   The observation of a correlation between task difficulty and optimal backtracking frequency is also important. It implies that training datasets must be tailored to the complexity of the problem being solved. This moves beyond a one-size-fits-all approach.

* The results on shuffled data during SFT provide strong evidence that RL requires strong internal consistency for effective training.

**Strengths:**

*   **Controlled experiments:**  The paper employs a strong experimental design with systematic variations in the number of backtracking steps and the correctness of SFT data.
*   **Synthetic data generation:** The use of synthetic datasets enables the authors to rigorously control the experimental conditions and isolate the influence of backtracking.
*   **Multiple reasoning tasks:**  The evaluation across eight diverse reasoning tasks enhances the generalizability of the findings (although still limited to the tasks at hand).
* The paper presents a nice collection of visualizations of rewards and accuracy across time.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited model scale:** The primary focus on Qwen2.5-3B limits the generalizability of the results to larger, more powerful LLMs.  While a Qwen2.5-7B Instruct comparison is given, a more diverse comparison with models of different sizes would be useful.

*   **Dataset scope:** The experiments are restricted to a set of reasoning tasks that, while diverse, may not fully capture the complexity of real-world reasoning scenarios.

*   **Synthetic data limitations:** While synthetic data provides control, it may not perfectly reflect the characteristics of human-generated reasoning data.  This could limit the applicability of the findings to models trained on real-world data.

*   **Reward function:** While the rule based reward functions provide a good learning signal, they might have unexpected interactions with back-tracking data.

* Limited discussion on negative examples during RL. The reward function allows for assigning a score during wrong output which could contribute to better model training during RL. The authors do not mention what they did here.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the development of more efficient and effective training strategies for LLMs used in reasoning tasks.  The findings could lead to:
*  Training data curation approaches that prioritize the structural patterns of reasoning traces (e.g., backtracking) rather than strictly focusing on accuracy.
* RL training strategies that adapt to the task difficulty by adjusting the level of backtracking in the SFT warm-up data.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:** While the paper provides interesting and well-supported insights into the interplay of SFT and RL, some limitations (particularly the limited model scale and dataset scope) temper its potential impact. Nevertheless, its systematic investigation of backtracking and the discovery that correctness is less important than structure (in the SFT warm-up) make it a notable contribution deserving careful consideration. Further work on larger models and more complex datasets could significantly increase the impact of these findings.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Mind the Quote: Enabling Quotation-Aware Dialogue in LLMs via Plug-and-Play Modules
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24292v1)
- **Authors**: Yueqi Zhang, Peiwen Yuan, Shaoxiong Feng, Yiwei Li, Xinglin Wang, Jiayi Shi, Chuyi Tan, Boyuan Pan, Yao Hu, Kan Li
- **Abstract**: Human-AI conversation frequently relies on quoting earlier text-"check it with the formula I just highlighted"-yet today's large language models (LLMs) lack an explicit mechanism for locating and exploiting such spans. We formalise the challenge as span-conditioned generation, decomposing each turn into the dialogue history, a set of token-offset quotation spans, and an intent utterance. Building on this abstraction, we introduce a quotation-centric data pipeline that automatically synthesises task-specific dialogues, verifies answer correctness through multi-stage consistency checks, and yields both a heterogeneous training corpus and the first benchmark covering five representative scenarios. To meet the benchmark's zero-overhead and parameter-efficiency requirements, we propose QuAda, a lightweight training-based method that attaches two bottleneck projections to every attention head, dynamically amplifying or suppressing attention to quoted spans at inference time while leaving the prompt unchanged and updating < 2.8% of backbone weights. Experiments across models show that QuAda is suitable for all scenarios and generalises to unseen topics, offering an effective, plug-and-play solution for quotation-aware dialogue.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Mind the Quote: Enabling Quotation-Aware Dialogue in LLMs via Plug-and-Play Modules":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of enabling Large Language Models (LLMs) to effectively handle quotations within conversations. The authors formalize this as a "span-conditioned generation" problem, breaking down each turn into the dialogue history, a set of token-offset quotation spans, and an intent utterance. To address this, they introduce a "quotation-centric data pipeline" for generating task-specific dialogues and verifying answer correctness.  They also propose QuAda, a lightweight, training-based method that dynamically amplifies or suppresses attention to quoted spans by attaching two bottleneck projections to every attention head.  The method updates a small percentage of backbone weights, leaving the prompt untouched. Experiments show QuAda's suitability across various scenarios, generalization to unseen topics, and effectiveness as a plug-and-play solution.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Problem Formulation:** The paper effectively formalizes a crucial yet often overlooked aspect of human-AI interaction: quotation-aware dialogue. Framing it as "span-conditioned generation" provides a clear and structured approach to addressing the problem.
*   **Data Pipeline:**  The automated data pipeline is a significant contribution. It allows for the generation of large-scale, task-specific dialogues with built-in correctness checks, crucial for training and evaluating models. The inclusion of human verification for the benchmark ensures high quality.
*   **Method (QuAda):** The proposed QuAda method is lightweight, parameter-efficient, and plug-and-play, making it practical for integrating into existing LLMs without substantial overhead. Dynamically adjusting attention based on quoted spans is a smart design choice.
*   **Diagnostic Tasks:** The decomposition of the problem into the five diagnostic tasks (Base, Multi-Span, Exclude, Info-Combine, Coref) provides a structured way to evaluate different aspects of the model's capabilities. This allows for targeted analysis and improvement.
*   **Empirical Results:** The experiments demonstrate QuAda's effectiveness across various scenarios, models, and scales. The ablation studies provide insights into the importance of both query and value modulation.
*   **Generalization:** The models are trained on different topics than tested, promoting quotation skills over content memorization.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Evaluation Scope:** While the five diagnostic tasks are well-defined, the scenarios remain somewhat artificial. It is unclear how well the method would perform in more complex, open-ended real-world conversations.
*   **Synthetic Data Bias:** Training on synthetic data generated by LLMs could introduce biases. Although the authors use human annotation to mitigate this risk, it is still a potential limitation.
*   **Lack of Comparison to State-of-the-Art Dialogue Models:** While the paper compares against strong baselines (Concat-Repeat, Marker-Insertion, Attention Steering), it does not compare with SOTA end-to-end dialogue models. Comparing with the SOTA dialogue models and also showing that the plug-and-play method can improve its performance would be a bonus.
*   **Overclaim on Human-LLM Interaction:** The claims regarding the substantial advancement of human-LLM interaction seem slightly overstated given the relatively controlled environment and focus on quotation handling.
*   **Zero Overhead for inference:** The model modifies attention mechanism scores, the zero overhead does not apply if the attention cache needs to be recomputed. The adapter layer in the value function side is a good approach but the claim should be revised.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of LLMs. The formalization of the "span-conditioned generation" problem, the automated data pipeline, and the lightweight QuAda method address a crucial gap in existing models' ability to handle quotations effectively. The framework offers a new direction for enabling human-like quotation awareness in LLMs and provides a valuable benchmark for future research.

The approach is novel, introducing a structured problem formulation and a parameter-efficient solution.  The automated data pipeline addresses a common bottleneck in LLM research: the availability of high-quality, task-specific training data.

The significance lies in its potential impact on human-AI conversation. By enabling LLMs to handle quotations more effectively, the method could improve the clarity, accuracy, and naturalness of interactions, paving the way for more intuitive and productive dialogues.

**Score:**

Score: 7.5

**Justification:**

The paper demonstrates strong technical contributions and addresses an important, yet under-explored, problem. The QuAda method exhibits desirable properties (lightweight, plug-and-play), and the results are compelling. However, the artificial nature of the evaluation scenarios, the potential for bias in the synthetic data, and the lack of evaluation with current dialogue models limit the score. Furthermore, the zero overhead statement on the inference is questionable. While significant, the practical impact and generalizability of the method need further validation in more realistic and complex conversational settings.

The data generation pipeline and QuAda can be a useful framework for other researchers in the community to explore in their downstream dialogue systems.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Large Language Models are Locally Linear Mappings
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24293v1)
- **Authors**: James R. Golden
- **Abstract**: We demonstrate that the inference operations of several open-weight large language models (LLMs) can be mapped to an exactly equivalent linear system for an input sequence without modifying the model weights or altering output predictions. Extending techniques from image diffusion models that exhibit local or piecewise linearity, we strategically alter the gradient computation with respect to a given input sequence for a next-token prediction such that the Jacobian of the model nearly exactly reproduces the forward prediction with a linear system. We demonstrate this approach across models (Llama 3, Gemma 3, Qwen 3, Phi 4, Mistral Ministral and OLMo 2, up to Llama 3.3 70B Q4) and show through the singular value decomposition of the detached Jacobian that these LLMs operate in extremely low-dimensional subspaces where many of the largest singular vectors decode to concepts related to the most-likely output token. This approach also allows us to examine the operation of each successive layer (and its attention and MLP components) as nearly-exact linear systems and observe the emergence of semantic concepts. Despite their expressive power and global nonlinearity, modern LLMs can be interpreted through nearly-exact locally linear decompositions that provide insights into their internal representations and reveal interpretable semantic structures in the next-token prediction process.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "Large Language Models are Locally Linear Mappings":

**Summary:**

The paper demonstrates that the inference operations of several open-weight large language models (LLMs) can be effectively represented as equivalent linear systems *locally* for a given input sequence. This is achieved by strategically altering the gradient computation (specifically, using detach operations) to freeze certain nonlinear activations, without modifying the model weights or changing the output prediction.  The authors analyze these linear systems via Singular Value Decomposition (SVD), showing that LLMs often operate in low-dimensional subspaces, where the dominant singular vectors correspond to interpretable semantic concepts related to the predicted next token. The approach is validated across various LLM architectures (Llama 3, Gemma, Qwen, Phi, Mistral, OLMo), and at different layer levels, offering insights into how semantic information is processed within the model. The paper also explores using the detached Jacobian as a conceptual steering operator.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core idea of approximating LLM inference locally as a linear mapping isn't entirely novel. Prior work, as the authors acknowledge, explored linearity in ReLU networks and image diffusion models. However, the *application* of this concept to modern transformer-based LLMs, the systematic methodology for achieving local linearity through detach operations on gated activations, and the extensive empirical validation across diverse models is a significant and valuable contribution. Applying SVD to the resulting Jacobian to reveal low-dimensional structure and interpretable concepts is a useful analytical technique. The concept of a "detached Jacobian" is a new idea within LLMs, even if the individual elements that make up the process have been touched on in other areas.

**Significance:** If substantiated by further research, the findings could have several significant implications:

*   **Interpretability:** Provides a new tool for understanding how LLMs process and represent information, potentially revealing which layers are responsible for specific semantic concepts, and how models perform next token prediction.
